The white boys of the town had reserved a swimming hole in the Withlacoochee River for their own exclusive use, and when they went to swim one day and found it filled with Negroes, young Holliday emptied his revolver among the darkies. One story says that he killed three and another that he only wounded that number. But as a result of this escapade, he disappeared from Valdosta and was never seen there again.
He was next heard of practising dentistry in Dallas. Having abandoned dentistry for gambling, he killed a man over a game of cards in Jacksboro. He killed a soldier a little later in another Texas town, and, to avoid capture by the military authorities, he rode alone across eight hundred miles of wild, unsettled country, swarming with hostile Indians, and arrived in Denver in 1876. There, in a knife duel, he almost killed Bud Ryan. He shot and seriously wounded Kid Kolton in Trinidad.

===Chinese===

他偶尔会从箱子里翻出一件念旧之物给挚友看——那是他西部漂泊一路带在身边的：一块旧马口铁招牌，上面漆着“J·D·霍利迪大夫——牙医”，从前挂在他达拉斯诊所门前。大夫常笑着想：像自己这样真正的游侠与冒险家，居然曾干过补龋齿、包牙冠的营生。
他是南方人，出身好，教养好，一口南方软塌塌的拖腔，高兴时也能做个儒雅绅士。什么良心上的顾忌，都不曾妨得着大夫忙碌的生活。对朋友他公道——朋友肯把最后一块钱托给他——可他的诚实分许多成色：方便的时候，他诚实；不方便，他便发一副做了手脚的牌。
胆气是他唯一的德行。他无所畏惧。尽管外表文弱、体力单薄，他那份冷静冷淡的神情里藏着警示的危险。枪柄上刻满道道的狠人——人人怕他们——他们自己也怕多克·霍利迪。
霍利迪生在佐治亚州瓦尔多斯塔，一门又古又好的南方世家，族中后人至今还住在佛罗里达州界以北的那座小城。他家祖祖辈辈种棉花、蓄奴隶；父亲在整个南北战争中是南军的少校。镇上白人小伙在威斯拉库奇河留了个专属泳潭，一天他们去游泳，发现潭里泡满了黑人；小霍利迪拔出左轮，朝黑小伙们打了个空。一说打死三个，一说只伤了三个。但经这场乱子，他从瓦尔多斯塔消失，再没在那儿露过面。
再听见他的音信，他已在达拉斯开业牙医。齐赌改行赌钱之后，他在杰克斯伯勒为一局牌杀了人。不久又在另一个得克萨斯镇子杀了个大兵；为躲避军方缉拿，他单人独骑穿过八百英里荒无人烟、遍布敌意印第安人的地界，1876 年抵丹佛。在丹佛一场刀斗里，他差点杀了巴德·瑞安。在特立尼达，他开枪重伤了基德·科尔顿。

===Original===

He quarrelled with Mike Gordon in Las Vegas and shot him dead. He drifted then into the Texas Panhandle, where he fell in with Wyatt Earp.
“I first met Doc Holliday in Fort Griffen, Texas, in 1877,” said Wyatt Earp. “I was down in that country from Dodge City after a bunch of cow thieves. There was a woman in town﻿—well, you can call her Doc’s inamorata. Her name was Kate Fisher, but in that refined town they called her Big Nose Kate. A name like that might suggest a big, coarse female, but Kate wasn’t like that; she was what you might call handsome, in a way. I liked Doc; he was a witty fellow and good company; he asked me a lot of questions about Dodge and thought he and Kate might like to come there. I had to go to Fort Clark for a few days, and when I got back to Fort Griffin, I found the town buzzing with excitement over Doc and Kate.
“Doc had sat in a game of poker one night with a fellow named Ed Bailey. This Bailey was a crooked card man, and he was always monkeying with the deadwood, which is to say, the discards, and Doc admonished him once or twice to quit this and play poker. Finally, a pretty nice pot came along. Doc called and Bailey spread down three kings. Doc didn’t say a word but just quietly pulled down the pot and threw his hand away without showing what he held. Of course, Bailey started a big holler.
“ ‘I saw you palming one of those kings,’ said Doc.
“ ‘Give me that money,’ roared Bailey. ‘I won it and I’ll have it.’
“Doc was stacking the chips in front of him as Bailey reached for them. Doc knocked his hand away. Bailey went for his six-shooter and was coming up with it when Doc drew a long knife from under his coat collar﻿—he had it hanging by a cord down his back﻿—and with a sidewise swipe below the brisket ripped Bailey wide open.
“Doc was arrested. While he was being held in the hotel office under guard of the town marshal and two policemen, Bailey’s gambler and cowboy friends gathered in the street. The crowd kept growing and getting uglier. There was talk of getting a rope, and things began to look pretty bad for Doc. Big Nose Kate got word of what was going on and hurried to the hotel. The officers let her in, and she had a little talk with Doc and went away. Now a big nose generally means a strong, bold character﻿—at least, so I’ve heard tell﻿—and right now Big Nose Kate lived up to her nose.

===Chinese===

他在拉斯维加斯同迈克·戈登闹翻，一枪把他毙了。随后他漂进得克萨斯锅柄区，在那里结识了怀亚特·厄普。
“我头回见多克·霍利迪是 1877 年，在得克萨斯的格里芬堡，”怀亚特·厄普说。“我从道奇城下来追一帮偷牛贼。镇上有个女人——唔，你可以叫她多克的情人。她叫凯特·费舍尔，可那座风雅的镇子管她叫大鼻子凯特。这名字听着像个粗壮的大娘们儿，凯特可不是；论标致，她也算得上。我中意多克；他俏皮，好相处；他拿道奇城问了我一大堆话，觉得他同凯特可以过去。我去克拉克堡待了几天，回格里芬堡，发现全镇正为多克同凯特的事炸了锅。
“一天夜里，多克同比一个叫埃德·贝利的打扑克。这贝利好在牌上做手脚，老摆弄‘死木’——就是垫牌——多克劝了他一两回，叫他规矩打牌。后来来了个不小的彩池。多克跟注，贝利摊下三张K。多克一言不发，静静把彩池拉过来，把手里的牌丢掉，不给看。当然，贝利嚷嚷开了。
“‘我燈见你掌心藏了一张K。’多克说。
“‘把钱给我，’贝利咆哮。‘我赢的，我就要。’
“贝利伸手抓筹码时，多克正把它们码在面前。多克一巴掌打开他的手。贝利抓六响枪，枪正往上抬，多克从衣领底下抽出一把长刀——那刀用绳拴着，吊在背上——横着一下，从胸口下沿把贝利划了个大开膛。
“多克被拘了。他在旅馆账房里由镇警长和两个警察看管着，贝利的赌徒牛仔朋友们聚在街上。人越聚越多，火气越来越大。有人说去拿绳子，多克的局面眼看不妙。大鼻子凯特得了信，急急赶到旅馆。警官们放她进去，她同多克小谈了几句，走了。大鼻子通常意味着性格强悍大胆——至少听人这么讲——眼下，大鼻子凯特就不负她这只鼻子。

===Original===

“She ordered a couple of horses saddled at a livery and hitched them in an alley. Then she started a blaze in a shed back of the hotel and ran into the street yelling, ‘Fire!’ When, just as she had figured, the mob deserted the front of the hotel and rushed to the burning building, Kate walked into the hotel office. This time she had on a man’s pants, coat, boots, and hat. From a satchel she was carrying she jerked a pair of six-shooters. Doc grabbed them and ordered the marshal and the two deputies to throw up their hands. While Doc kept the marshals covered, Kate disarmed them, and, stowing their artillery in her bag, she and Doc backed out of the door.
“When the mob got back from the fire, madder than ever and still talking about a lynching, Doc and Kate were galloping hard across the prairies on a four-hundred-mile ride to Dodge City. I found them there on my return, living in style at a hotel, and they laughed as they told me about their adventure and seemed to regard it as a fine joke.”
Between Wyatt Earp and Doc Holliday, as cold, deadly men, perhaps, as the frontier knew, existed a friendship classic in its loyalty. For his friend, the coldest-blooded killer in Tombstone risked his life time and again, and only the accidents of the fighting prevented his making friendship’s last supreme sacrifice. And what Doc Holliday gave in friendship, Wyatt Earp returned in a friendship as staunch.
“Doc Holliday saved my life in Dodge City once,” said Wyatt Earp. “I had arrested a man known to be dangerous. His friends surrounded me to rescue him. Doc saw one of these cutthroats draw a gun and throw down to shoot me in the back. ‘Look out, Wyatt,’ Doc shouted. But before the words were out of his mouth, he jerked his six-shooter and shot the man who in another second would have murdered me. Lifelong friendships on the frontier were founded on such incidents as this. Doc was my friend and I was Doc’s friend until he died. Many a man told me this and that about Doc Holliday﻿—and he was no saint﻿—but nobody on earth could knock Doc Holliday to me. He was one of the finest, cleanest men in the world, though, of course, he was a little handy with his gun and had to kill a few fellows.”

===Chinese===

“她到一家车马行叫人备了两匹马，拴进一条小巷。随后她在旅馆后面的棚子里点了一把火，跑到街上大喊‘着火啦！’不出她所料，人群撇下旅馆正面，涌向那座冒烟的房子；凯特走进旅馆账房。这回她穿着男人的裤子、外套、靴子，戴着男人帽子。她从手提包里掠出两支六响枪。多克一把抓过，喝令警长和两个副手举手。多克枪管着警官，凯特缴了他们的械，把家伙塞进自己包里；两人倒退出门。
“等人群救完火回来，火气更旺，又嚷着要吊人，多克同凯特早已纵马疾驰，四百英里直奔道奇城。我回来时在那儿找到了他们，体体面面住着旅馆；他们讲起这场冒险直笑，只当一桩妙谈。”
怀亚特·厄普同多克·霍利迪——也许是边疆所知最冷酷、最致命的一对人——之间，存在着一场以忠诚著称的典范友谊。为了朋友，这位墓碑镇最冷血的杀手一次次性命相搏，只因枪战中的种种偶然，才没能献上友谊的最后牺牲。而霍利迪付出多少友情，怀亚特·厄普便以同样坚定的友情回偿。
“多克·霍利迪在道奇城救过我一条命，”怀亚特·厄普说。“我抓了个出了名的危险人物。他的朋友们围上来要抢人。多克燈见其中一个刀客拔枪要照我背后打。‘留神，怀亚特，’多克喊。话音未落，他已拔出六响枪，一枪撂倒了那再过一秒就要我命的人。边疆上生死不渝的交情，就是这样结下的。多克是我的朋友，我是多克的朋友，直到他死。许多人跟我说过多克·霍利迪的这样那样——他不是圣人——可天下没人能在我面前说多克坏话。他是世上最好、最干净的人之一，当然，他枪上利索了点，不得不杀几个家伙。”

===Original===

So these two friends stood side by side through good and evil report, through fortune and misfortune, through thick and thin, and back to back they fought in the swirling gloom of the final storm that threatened to engulf them.
Virgil, Morgan, James, and Warren Earp, his brothers, soon joined Wyatt in Tombstone. Of the five Earps, Virgil was the eldest, Wyatt, Morgan, James, and Warren following in descending age sequence. Though Wyatt was the second born, he was the undisputed leader of the fraternal quintette by right of brains, initiative, and force of character. All were stalwart blond men, the family resemblance strong in every face, all plainly whelped in the same lion’s litter. Wyatt, Virgil, and Morgan alone played important roles in Tombstone’s colourful drama, James and Warren being looked upon by their elder brothers as mere cubs, promising, perhaps, but of an age too tender for the game of fang and claw.
Virgil Earp, who rose to historic importance as town marshal of Tombstone, had fought through the Civil War in the Union Army, was old to the frontier, and had himself served as early marshal of Dodge City. He had the stern look of a soldier and a soldier’s stern sense of duty. He was a taciturn man of rough simplicity and blunt honesty, moving toward whatever destination he had in view with crushing inevitability and stolid, blind fearlessness. Morgan Earp was more headstrong and impetuous and mentally keener. With the cool poise that was a distinguishing mark of the breed, he, too, was slow to wrath, but when once aroused, fought with the same desperate, merciless courage that distinguished his two older brothers. He also had been schooled to the frontier in Dodge City.
When the railroad went through Arizona and the new town of Benson became the depot for all incoming and outgoing freight for the entire Tombstone region, Wyatt Earp for seven months rode as shotgun messenger for the Wells-Fargo Company on the Benson stages. With stages being robbed with startling frequency on all the other lines centering in Tombstone, it might be supposed that these seven months were crowded with thrilling adventures. But, on the contrary, nothing at all happened. When highwaymen saw the grim Dodge City fighter sitting up on the box with a shotgun across his knees, they left that stage alone.

===Chinese===

这两个朋友就这样并肩站着，好名声坏名声、顺境逆境，生死与共；当最后那场要把他们吞没的风暴旋起黑暗时，他们背靠着背作战。
弟弟们——维尔吉尔、摩根、詹姆斯和沃伦·厄普——很快来墓碑镇同怀亚特会合。厄普家五兄弟，维尔吉尔最长，以下依次是怀亚特、摩根、詹姆斯、沃伦。怀亚特虽行二，凭头脑、魄力和性格之力，是五兄弟当仁不让的领袖。五人个个魁梧金发，每张脸上都印着强烈的家族相似——分明是同一窝狮崽。在墓碑镇的锦绣大戏里，只有怀亚特、维尔吉尔和摩根担纲要角；詹姆斯和沃伦在哥哥们眼里不过是两只小崽子，或许有出息，但玩牙爪的游戏年纪还嫌嫩。
维尔吉尔·厄普——日后以墓碑镇镇警长之职名留史册——整个南北战争在北军中打过来，是边疆老人，早年还做过道奇城警长。他有军人的严容和军人严峻的责任感。他沉默寡言，粗直简朴，认准目标便以压倒的必然性和盲目的无畏闷头前进。摩根·厄普更倔更念，脑子更快。同这一家标志性的冷静沉着一样，他也不轻易动怒；可一旦发作，打起来同两位哥哥一样亡命、一样无情。他也在道奇城受过边疆的熏陶。
铁路贯通亚利桑那后，新镇本森成了整个墓碑镇地区货运的枢纽；怀亚特·厄普随后七个月在本森驿车上替韦尔斯-法戈公司做押运哨兵。在以墓碑镇为中心的其他各条线路上，驿车被动得惊人地频繁；照理说这七个月该是惊险叠出。可恰恰相反，什么也没发生。路盜们燈见那位面色阴沉的道奇城斗士端坐车夫座上，霰弹枪横在膝头，便绕开那班车。
怀亚特·厄普当班的七个月里，本森驿车一辆也没遭劫。记住这个事实。它将成为日后某些事件的一条照亮的注脚。

===Original===

Not a single Benson stage was robbed during Wyatt Earp’s term of service. Bear this fact in mind. It will prove an illuminating footnote to certain events that happened later.
While Wyatt Earp was guarding Wells-Fargo treasure boxes, the Oriental saloon and gambling house, then the largest in Tombstone, got into difficulties. Dave Rickabaugh, one of the owners, had offended a clique of gamblers who were trying, by threats and gunplay, to run the proprietors out of town. Luke Short, an old-time Dodge City friend of Wyatt Earp’s, was dealing in the Oriental. He was a quiet, gentlemanly fellow who, despite his diminutive stature, had a reputation as a gun fighter. Charlie Storms, formerly of Deadwood, one of the opposition gamblers, ugly and half drunk, came in.
“Any man who’ll work for Rickabaugh is a yellow coyote,” he snarled at Luke. Luke slid a case card out of the box and said nothing.
“You goin’ to sit there and take that?” sneered Storms. Luke took a yellow stack off the five-spot and set it in the rack.
“These Dodge City fighters ain’t deuce high here in Tombstone,” taunted Storms. Luke paid off a bet on the queen.
Friends dragged Storms away; Luke went out to lunch. When Short returned, Storms stood in the doorway of the Oriental with a six-shooter in his hand.
“Say, you Dodge City killer, come on and fight,” roared Storms. “I’ll give you the first shot.” Short took the first shot. Then two more for good measure. Storms fell dead.
But the killing, for which Short was acquitted in Tucson﻿—he never returned to Tombstone﻿—did not stop the campaign of intimidation. As a last resort, the proprietors of the Oriental offered Wyatt Earp a partnership in the place. He accepted. The new partner took over the management of the house. He moved among the patrons with a genial smile and a pair of big six-shooters buckled around him. He sent this message to the firm’s enemies:
“If you fellows are still looking for trouble, drop into the Oriental and I’ll fill you so full that it’ll run out of your ears. I aim to please.”
The sight of the gaunt old lion ready and perhaps eager for battle, discouraged the gamblers of the hostile faction. They thought him, as one expressed it, “too gosh-darned accommodatin’.” There were no more attempts at terrorism. The Oriental became the most orderly place in town. Also one of the most popular and prosperous.

===Chinese===

怀亚特·厄普押着韦尔斯-法戈的钱箱时，墓碑镇当时最大的东方酒馆赌场出了麻烦。股东之一的戴夫·里克博得罪了一帮赌徒，那帮人正用威胁和枪火想把东家们赶出镇子。卢克·肖特，怀亚特·厄普道奇城时代的老友，在东方馆发牌。他是个安静斯文的小个子，个子虽矮，却有枪客的名头。对立赌徒中的一个，从前在死木镇的查利·斯托姆斯，丑者张脸，带着七分酒意进来了。
“给里克博干活的都是黄毛郊狼。”他冲卢克狺狺。卢克从牌盒里抽出一张牌，一言不发。
“你就坐着听下去？”斯托姆斯讥笑。卢克从五点上取下一摞黄码，插回码架。
“道奇城的打手，到了墓碑镇连两块钱都不值。”斯托姆斯返他。卢克赔了王后上的一注。
“朋友们把斯托姆斯拖走了；卢克出去吃午饭。肖特回来时，斯托姆斯手里握着六响枪，堵在东方馆门口。
“喂，道奇城杀人犯，出来打一场！”斯托姆斯咆哮。“先给你一枪的便宜。”肖特要了这一枪。又补了两枪。斯托姆斯倒地毙命。
可是这场枪杀——肖特在图森获判无罪，从此再没回墓碑镇——并没有吓退恐吓战。走投无路，东方馆的东家们请怀亚特·厄普入伙。他应了。新股东接手馆务，周旋于客人之间，面带亲切的笑，腰间别着两支大六响枪。他给东家的敌人们捎了句话：
“你们几位要是还在找麻烦，到东方馆来，我管你们管到从耳朵里溢出来。顾客至上。”
老狮子那副随时应战、也许巴不得一战的样子，叫敌对赌徒们气短。用其中一位的话说：“太他蚂地热心周到了。”恐吓再没有下文。东方馆成了全城最有规矩的场子，也是最红火、最兴隆的场子之一。

===Original===

As part proprietor of a big saloon and gambling house, Wyatt Earp seemed on the road to affluence. He resigned his position with the express company, and Morgan Earp succeeded him as shotgun messenger on the Benson stages.
Bat Masterson, former sheriff of Dodge City, joined Wyatt Earp in Tombstone but remained only a few months. Bat returned to Dodge City when he learned that his brother, Jim, was having trouble with George Peacock and Al Updegraft. Peacock and Jim Masterson were partners in the Lady Gay saloon and dance hall in Dodge, and Updegraft was their bartender. Bat went by stage to Deming, where he struck the railroad. As he dropped off the train in Dodge, he saw Peacock and Updegraft coming toward the depot and opened fire. Updegraft got a bullet through the lungs and staggered back into the dance hall. Peacock dodged behind a calaboose across the railroad tracks, and Bat behind a box car, and they shot it out until their ammunition was exhausted. Neither Bat nor Peacock was injured. Updegraft was in bed for two months, and when pneumonia set in, he died.
“Bat Masterson was a brave man,” said Wyatt Earp, whose estimate of the famous sheriff is of interest. “As a peace officer he deserved all the celebrity he ever had, but as a killer his reputation has been greatly exaggerated. Old stories credit Bat with having killed twenty-seven men. The truth is, he never killed but four in his life.
“I knew Bat when he was a kid in Wichita and he and I were bosom friends in Dodge. I hunted buffalo with him when he was hardly big enough to handle a rifle and I’ve been with him on many a roundup after outlaws. I shared the same home with him in Denver after my Tombstone days were over and kept up a correspondence with him until his death a few years ago in New York. I know his career from beginning to end.
“Bat killed his first man at Sweetwater, Texas, when he was eighteen years old. While he was serving as a civilian scout with the army, a dance hall girl in Sweetwater took a shine to him. A cavalry sergeant named King grew jealous. When King went into the dance hall one night and saw Bat and the girl dancing together, he drew his gun. The girl saw him first and threw her arms around Bat to protect him. King’s bullet killed her and, passing through her body, wounded Bat in the thigh. As the girl fell, Bat put a bullet through King’s heart.

===Chinese===

身为大酒馆赌场的股东，怀亚特·厄普眼看要发达了。他辞了捷运公司的差，摩根·厄普接他做了本森驿车的押运哨兵。
道奇城前警长巴特·马斯特森来墓碑镇同怀亚特·厄普汇合，只待了几个月。巴特听说弟弟吉姆同乔治·皮科克和阿尔·厄普德格拉夫闹了纠纷，便回道奇城去了。皮科克同吉姆·马斯特森在道奇城合伙开“盖伊小姐”酒馆舞厅，厄普德格拉夫是他们雇的酒保。巴特乘驿车到明令，接上铁路。火车进道奇城，他一跳下车，燈见皮科克同厄普德格拉夫正朝车站走来，当即开火。厄普德格拉夫肺上挨了一枪，踉跄着退回舞厅。皮科克躲到铁路对面监狱后头，巴特躲到一节棚车后，两人枪来弹往，直到子弹打光。巴特同皮科克都没伤着。厄普德格拉夫躺了两个月，引发肺炎，死了。
“巴特·马斯特森是条勇敢的汉子，”怀亚特·厄普说——他对这位名警长的评价值得一听。“当执法官，他配得上他得过的一切名声；可论杀人，他的名声给夸张得太厉害。老故事说他杀了二十七个人。真相是：他一辈子只杀过四个。
“巴特在威奇托还是娃娃时我就认得他；在道奇城我们是心腹之交。他小得还扛不动步枪时我就同他猎过野牛；追缉盜匪的民团我同他出过不知多少回。墓碑镇岁月之后，我在丹佛同他同住一个屋檐下；直到几年前他死在纽约，我们通信不断。他的履历，我从头到尾都清楚。
“巴特头一个杀的是在得克萨斯斯威特沃特，那年他十八岁。他在军中做平民侦察兵，斯威特沃特一个舞厅姑娘看上了他。一个叫金的骑兵中士拈了醋。一天夜里，金走进舞厅，燈见巴特同姑娘跳舞，拔了枪。姑娘先燈见他，张臂抱住巴特护他。金的子弹打死了她，穿身而过，伤了巴特的大腿。姑娘倒下时，巴特一枪打穿了金的心口。

===Original===

“When I was marshal of Dodge in 1877, I went out with a posse to hunt some train robbers who had held up a Santa Fe express at Kinsley, and in my absence, Ed Masterson, Bat’s brother, acted as marshal. Some Texas cowboys were raising cain in a dance hall, and Bat and Ed went to quiet the disturbance. At the door, Jack Wagner, one of the cowboys, shot and killed Ed Masterson. As Ed reeled out into the street, Bat killed Wagner. When Alf Walker, the ringleader of the cowboys, came rushing out of the hall with a six-shooter Bat shot him twice. Walker ran up the street and through a saloon and dropped dead in an alley. Bat went into the dance hall and opened up on the other cowboys, but they piled out through the windows and got away.
“The next man on Bat’s list was Updegraft. Bat showed me the message he received in Tombstone and it was not from his brother. It didn’t say what kind of trouble Jim Masterson was mixed up in. It only said that Peacock and Updegraft were threatening to kill him. Bat had had a quarrel with Jim and was hardly on speaking terms with him at the time but he took the first stage out of Tombstone to go back to Dodge and help his brother. That was the old frontier’s brand of loyalty. These four﻿—King, Wagner, Walker, and Updegraft﻿—are the only men Bat ever killed, and there has always been a doubt whether Updegraft died of pneumonia or the wound from Bat’s bullet.”
The Tombstone stage was set and the drama getting under way when John H. Behan, his entrance slightly delayed, as befitted that of so important an actor, made his bow as the first sheriff of Cochise County. He was appointed to the position by the governor when Cochise County was organized out of Pima County in the winter of 1880﻿–﻿1881, and Tombstone was made the county seat. Behan had lived in Arizona since 1836, having come across the plains from Missouri, had had varied frontier experience, and had served as recorder and sheriff of Yavapai county.
Already in middle life, he was a bustling, self-important man, never too busy to stop and shake hands or clap somebody on the shoulder with a great show of friendliness. Everybody in Tombstone was soon calling him Johnny Behan.
Johnny Behan was a good man misplaced in a tough mining camp, his life and character twisted by circumstances into which he had been hurled by one of destiny’s unfortunate accidents.

===Chinese===

“1877 年我当道奇警长时，带民团出去追一伙在金斯利抢圣菲捷车的劫车贼；我不在，由巴特的弟弟埃德·马斯特森代警长。几个得克萨斯牛仔在舞厅里闹事，巴特同埃德去弹压。在门口，牛仔之一的杰克·瓦格纳开枪打死了埃德·马斯特森。埃德搖搖晃晃走到街上倒下时，巴特打死了瓦格纳。牛仔头目阿尔夫·沃克持六响枪从舞厅里冲出来，巴特两枪打中他。沃克沿街跑过一家酒馆，死在一条小巷里。巴特进舞厅朝其余牛仔开火，他们从窗户爬出去跑了。
“巴特名单上的下一个就是厄普德格拉夫。他在墓碑镇接到的信我看过，不是他弟弟发的。信上没说吉姆·马斯特森卷进了什么麻烦，只说皮科克同厄普德格拉夫放话要杀他。巴特同吉姆吵过架，当时几乎不说话，可他还是搭上头一班驿车回道奇城帮弟弟。这就是老边疆式的义气。金、瓦格纳、沃克、厄普德格拉夫——巴特杀过的就这四个；而且厄普德格拉夫到底是死于肺炎还是死于巴特那颗子弹，从来是个疑案。”
墓碑镇的舞台搭好、大戏开场之际，约翰·H·比汉——出场略迟，正如如此重要的演员所应有的——登台亮相，做了科奇斯县首任警长。1880 至 1881 年冬，科奇斯县从皮马县析出、墓碑镇定为县治，他由州长委任此职。比汉从密苏里越大平原来亚利桑那，居留已久，边疆历练种种都经过，做过亚瓦派县的登记官和警长。
已入中年，他是个忙忙碌碌、自视甚高的人，再忙也忘不了停下来握手，或大表示友好地拍人肩膀。墓碑镇人很快都叫他约翰尼·比汉。
约翰尼·比汉是个投错了硬矿镇的好人，他的性命和品性，都被命运一次不幸的意外抛进去的境遇担坏了。

===Original===

In a happier environment, he would have been considered a splendid character. He was sunny, companionable, kindly, honest, generous, loyal. But in Tombstone he was a twenty-two calibre man in a forty-five calibre town. Tombstone thirsted for strong waters; John Behan had only sarsaparilla and lemon pop on tap.
He was not devoid of courage or initiative. But his indecision left an impression of timidity, and he lacked the force to finish what he started. He was vacillating, flustered, impulsive. He darted at a problem instead of calmly thinking it out. He had energy but clouded vision. He was a man of words rather than action. He blustered and failed to live up to his bluster. He talked like a big man and acted like a little one. He was perhaps not wholly inoffensive and free from guile but, judged by his Tombstone career, he seems to have been rather futile. However, Johnny Behan was nobody’s fool. He knew a cholla from a prickly pear when the desert wind was southerly.
But if, as a man, Johnny Behan was small potatoes, as a politician he was some pumpkins. Canny and sly and very crafty was the sheriff at the deep and subtle game of politics. He knew every crook and turn and sharp angle of it and was a wizard at all the tricks. When he smiled a little more than usual, people used to say, “Johnny’s probably up to some top-and-bottom trick or dealing from a marked deck.” Or if his face was particularly lamblike and innocent, he was suspected of having an ace up his sleeve. No amateur politician had any business sitting in at Johnny Behan’s game. The man who gambled with the sheriff at politics had to nurse his chips and play his cards close to his belly, or he would find himself pushing his chair back from the table without bed money or the price of a breakfast. As a politician, Johnny Behan was a foxy, dangerous boy.
Sheriff Behan was hardly more than comfortably settled in office when Wyatt Earp announced himself a candidate for sheriff at the next election. So the two men were political rivals from the first, and political rivalry developed into personal enmity. Friendly with Sheriff Behan and solidly arrayed against the Earps were Curly Bill and his outlaws. After Wyatt Earp had on one occasion knocked Curly Bill about the head with the butt of a six-shooter and dragged him off to jail, the freebooter chieftain nursed his wrath and thirsted for revenge.

===Chinese===

换一个好些的环境，他本会被看作一流人品：开朗，随和，厚道，诚实，慷慨，讲义气。可在墓碑镇，他是个二十二口径的人，落在了四十五口径的镇子上。墓碑镇要喝烈酒；约翰·比汉桶里只有沙士水和柠檬汽水。
他不缺胆量，也不缺闯劲。可他的犹豫不决给人怯懦的印象，他办事有头无力。他反复无常，慌手慌脚，冲动行事。他扑向问题，而不是冷静想透。他有干劲，眼光却发昏。他是言语的人，不是行动的人。他吹牛，又达不到吹出去的牛。他说大话，做小事。他未必全然无喜无害，但以他在墓碑镇的履历看，他算得上无能。不过，约翰尼·比汉不是任何人的傻瓜。沙漠风朝南刮时，他也分得清仙人掌和刺梨。
可是，做人，约翰尼·比汉是个小土豆；做政客，他可是个大南瓜。在政治这场深奥的细戏里，这位警长精明、诡猾、老辣。这门学问的每一道弯每一个锐角他都门儿清，十八般伎俩样样是行家。他笑得比平常多一点，人们就说：“约翰尼怕是又在玩顶底换牌，或者用做了记号的牌局发牌。”他脸上格外羔羊般无辜，人家就疑他袖子里藏了A。业余政客可没资格上约翰尼·比汉的牌桌。同警长玩政治的，得看好筹码、把牌贴着肚皮打，不然一觉醒来就发现自己推椅离桌，连过夜钱和早饭钱都不剩。论政治，约翰尼·比汉是只危险的小狐狸。
比汉警长在位子上刚坐热，怀亚特·厄普便宣布竞选下届警长。于是两人从一开始就是政敌，政争又酸成了私仇。同比汉警长友好、同厄普家誓不两立的，是卷毛比尔和他的盜匪们。自从怀亚特·厄普有一回用六响枪柄敲了卷毛比尔的脑袋、把他拖去坐牢，这位匪首便怀恨在心，日夜思复仇。

===Original===

Sharing his deadly antipathy were such leading figures in the outlaw confederacy as Ike, Finn, and Billy Clanton, ferocious Old Man Clanton, their father, Tom and Frank McLowery, John Ringo, Pete Spence, and Frank Stilwell.
A power to be dreaded and reckoned with were these outlaws. They were strong in numbers, and most of the dwellers up and down the lonely reaches of the San Pedro, Sulphur Springs, and San Simon valleys were either their avowed friends and allies or maintained a show of friendliness through fear. If the Earps ruled in Tombstone, all the wild region in which Tombstone was an oasis lay under the shadow of outlaw dominance and was, it might be said, in fief to these murderous and powerful bands. The hostility of the outlaws, countenanced, it might seem, by Sheriff Behan’s friendly attitude toward them, finally brought in the day when the power of the Earps was broken and they withdrew from Tombstone forever, leaving behind them a crimson trail to mark their vengeance.
This, then, was the lineup in a situation that rapidly developed into hair-trigger tension. On one side Sheriff Behan and his puissant political machine, with murderous outlaw hatreds in the shadowy background; on the other side, Wyatt, Virgil, and Morgan Earp and Doc Holliday, as the fighting force of the Earp faction, entrenched in power in Tombstone but ringed with enemies who watched and waited unseen in the deserts and mountains.
Wyatt Earp cleaned up Tombstone. His motives may have been those of an honest champion of good government, or they may have been those of a self-seeking politician establishing a record on which he might stand for election in the shrievalty contest. One’s choice is optional in the matter. But the fact remains that he did a good job. His faction became known as the Law and Order party. He had the support of the best citizens. The Tombstone papers of the period were outspoken in his praise.
Sheriff Behan’s administration, on the other hand, was repeatedly and severely criticized. His complacent relations with the outlaws particularly brought about his head the protests of citizens and the denunciations of the press. It is not surprising, in view of the bitterness of the political situation, that all the charges made against the Earps had their origin in Sheriff Behan’s faction.

===Chinese===

同他共这份死仇的，还有匪盟的中坚人物：艾克、芬和比利·克兰顿兄弟，他们凶暴的父亲老克兰顿，汤姆同弗兰克·麦克罗里，约翰·林戈，皮特·斯彭斯，弗兰克·斯蒂尔韦尔。
这班盜匪是一股不可小觑、必须掂量的势力。他们人多势众；圣佩德罗、硫磺泉、圣西蒙三条谷地僻野上下的住户，多半不是他们公开的朋友盟友，便是慑于淫威维持着友善的表面。如果说厄普家统治着墓碑镇，那么墓碑镇这片绿洲四外的整个荒蛮地界，都罩在盜匪的阴影下，可以说，是这几个凶恶强大匪帮的采邑。盜匪的敌意——看比汉警长对他们的亲善态度，似乎是得了默许——终于招来了那一天：厄普家的势力被打垮，永远退出了墓碑镇，身后留下一条血红的复仇之路。
这便是迅速绷紧到一触即发之势的阵营对垒。一边是比汉警长和他强大的政治机器，背后暗影里是杀人的匪仇；另一边是怀亚特、维尔吉尔、摩根·厄普同多克·霍利迪——厄普阵营的战斗主力——在墓碑镇据权而守，四周却被看不见的仇敌在荒漠群山间窥伺着、等待着。
怀亚特·厄普把墓碑镇整肃了。他的动机，可以说是一个善良政府捍卫者的动机，也可以说是一个自谋前程的政客在攒竞选本钱——这随你选。但事实是：他干得漂亮。他的阵营被称为“法治党”。他得到最好公民的支持。那一时期的墓碑镇报纸直言不讳地赞扬他。
比汉警长的施政则屡遭严词抨击。他同盜匪们怡然自得的交情，尤其招来公民的抗议和报界的痛斥。考虑到政局的怨毒，也就难怪：一切针对厄普家的指控，都发源于比汉警长的阵营。

===Original===

The desperate character of Wyatt Earp and his colleagues was pointed out. It was hinted that the Law and Order party was controlled by gunmen bent only upon their own aggrandizement. Vague rumours were set going that the Earps were in collusion with stage robbers. Suspicion was aroused in the public mind, but with the machinery of the law in his control, Sheriff Behan took no steps to arrest or bring to trial the men against whom these whispered insinuations were directed.
The long-smouldering feud between the two men might have been comic if its results had been less tragic. Wyatt Earp ignored the little sheriff or roused himself only occasionally to make a lazy gesture as if brushing a fly from the end of his nose. It was as if a truculent pygmy with twanging bow were shooting pins at some drowsy old saga giant. But when Johnny Behan fell back upon the ingratiating tactics of a politician, the advantage was all on his side. Johnny Behan was friendly, Wyatt Earp was grim; Johnny Behan smiled, Wyatt Earp shot from the hip; Johnny Behan was the personification of the glad hand, Wyatt Earp the personification of the trigger finger.﻿ ﻿… A lion stalks with measured stride through the dewy shadows. Back of him is the darkness of the donga. The moon is a sickle of silver in a glowing green sky against which a tall palm leans blackly. The grunting roar of the old wilderness monarch rolls with dim, menacing clamour across the veldt. From the mimosa thickets a fox fills the twilight with a thin uproar of shrill, savage barks.

===Chinese===

人家指出怀亚特·厄普及其同伙的亡命品性；暗示“法治党”不过受一帮只图自肥的枪客操控；还放出含含糊糊的流言，说厄普家同劫车贼勾结。公众心里起了疑；可执法机器握在比汉警长手里，对这些耳语所指向的人，他一步也没采取拘捕或审判的措施。
这两个人的长期暗斗，若不是结局那样惨，本可算一出喜剧。怀亚特·厄普不把小警长放在眼里，偶尔懒懒抬抬手，像掸鼻子尖上的一只苍蝇。好比一个怒气冲冲的侏儒，抡着弓弦睡啪响，朝一位打盹的老巨人射大头针。可约翰尼·比汉一退回政客那套讨好的功夫，便宜全在他那边。约翰尼·比汉友好，怀亚特·厄普阴沉；约翰尼·比汉微笑，怀亚特·厄普拔枪就打；约翰尼·比汉是“热络握手”的化身，怀亚特·厄普是“扳机手指”的化身。……狮子踏着带露的暗影，迈着匀步巡行。它背后是峡谷的黑暗。发绿的亮天上悬着一钩银月，映着一株高棕榈的黑影。老荒野之主的咆哮，带着昏沉的威吓，滚滚漫过草原。含羞树丛里，一只胡狼用尖利狂吠搅满了黄昏。

## XXI Hands Up! / 二十一 举起手来！
===Original===

Bisbee is a city, you might say, of only one dimension﻿—length. It lies like a piece of string along the bottom of a narrow canyon in the Mule Mountains. Its main street in the bed of the winding slit is miles long with handsome business blocks near the middle of the thread and good-looking residences out at the ends. Of course, if one wishes to be precise, there is a little width and a little height to the town, and the height up the canyon walls is about as great as the width across the canyon. The residences hang on the steeps as if glued there. A business man going home to lunch journeys straight up by a series of ladderlike steps that bear street names. If you enjoy mountain climbing, Bisbee will appeal to you as an ideal place of residence. If you happen to be fat, you will fall into a panic at mere sight of the town.
Under the main street is a giant subway to drain off the water when it rains. After a storm, the water roars through the subterranean sluice like an express train and flows out in a spreading flood on the plains at the south end of the canyon. It is surprising what a seething torrent the tiny drops of a light rain will form when they trickle down the mountainsides and merge at the bottom of the canyon. If it were not for this subway, a light shower might wash the town away.
Right in town at the edge of the business centre is the Copper Queen mine. It seems, in a way, to merge with the stores. You pass a soda fountain and a haberdashery shop with windows full of shirts and come to one of the richest copper mines in the world. Crushed in between its mountain walls, this bizarre little city of 22,000 people is the metropolis of southeastern Arizona. Twenty-six miles north is Tombstone. An equal distance south are Douglas and the Mexican border.
Bisbee, with its schools and churches and wealth and culture, has travelled a long road since its boom days as a mining camp in the early ’eighties when copper was struck in the Mules. Then it was a rough town full of boozing kens and brothels and dance halls, and Brewery Gulch was famous as a rendezvous for hard characters from the four corners of the frontier. But it was a busy, moneymaking place, too, and from the first it grew steadily and solidly.

===Chinese===

比斯比可以说是一座只有一维的城市——长度。它像一根细绳，躺在骡山一条窄峡谷的谷底。主街位于蜿蜒的谷缝底部，绵延数英里，漂亮的商铺大楼聚在绳子中段，体面的住宅散在两端。当然，若要较真，这城也有一点点宽度和一点点高度，而沿峡谷壁向上的高度，大致与峡谷横越的宽度相当。住宅贴在陡坡上，仿佛粘上去的。商人回家吃午饭，得循着一道道带街名的梯子般的台阶直上直下。你若喜爱登山，比斯比在你眼里便是理想的居所。你若碰巧体型发福，光看一眼这座城就会心里发慌。
主街底下有一条巨大的地下暗渠，用来在下雨时排水。暴雨过后，水在地下泄洪道里奔腾，声如快车，涌出峡谷南端，在平原上漫成一片洪流。一场细雨的点点水滴，顺山坡淌下、在谷底汇合，竟能形成那样一条翻滚的激流，实在惊人。若没有这条暗渠，一阵小雨也许就把整座城冲走了。
就在城里、商业区边上，是铜皇后矿。在某种意义上，它似乎同店铺连成了一体。你走过一个冷饮柜、一家橱窗里摆满衬衫的服饰店，就来到了世界上最富的铜矿之一。这座挤在山壁之间的古怪小城有2.2万人口，是亚利桑那东南部的都会。往北26英里是墓碑镇；往南同样的路程是道格拉斯和墨西哥国界。
比斯比有学校、教堂、财富与文化，自从十九世纪八十年代初骡山发现铜矿、它作为矿业营地的暴发岁月以来，已经走过了漫长的路。那时它是一座粗野的城，满街酒馆、妓院和舞厅，酿酒沟以边疆四方悍匪的聚点而闻名。但它也是个忙碌的、挣钱的地方，而且从一开始就稳步而扎实地成长。

===Original===

It was like a piece of string in that yesterday of long ago, as it is today, and its one-story frame business houses and shanty saloons were strung out up and down the canyon in twin rows along its single winding street, while the mountainsides were plastered with the precariously clinging pine shacks of miners. Among the merchandising establishments of that pioneer time, none did a more flourishing business than the general store of Goldwater & Casteñeda.
Dan Dowd and Red Sample, rough-looking customers in their cowboy paraphernalia, stood in front of Goldwater & Casteñeda’s store at half past seven o’clock on the night of December 8, 1883. The Christmas shopping season was in full swing and Bisbee’s main street was bright with street lamps and the light shining from store windows.
“All set?” asked Dan Dowd out of the corner of his mouth.
“Yep,” replied Red Sample.
“Then let her rip.”
Whereupon these two hard-looking customers slid six-shooters from the scabbards at their sides and one shot up the street and the other shot down.
The street a moment before had been, in a manner of speaking, full. A moment afterward, the street, as you might say, was empty. Shoppers on the sidewalk ran into the stores. Shoppers in the stores, though curious, stayed there. Dan Dowd kept on firing up the street, and Red Sample kept on firing down.
As their bullets were singing Christmas carols on the sidewalk, three friends and business associates, Tex Howard, Bill Delaney, and Dan Kelly, were inside the store doing their Christmas shopping with six-shooters. While proprietors, clerks, and customers stood lined along the walls with hands in the air, the trio rifled the cash drawers, ransacked the safe, looted the show cases of watches and jewellery, and scooping everything into a sack, rejoined their companions in the street.
Meanwhile, Dan Dowd and Red Sample had been having a busy time. When their first shots had sent the street crowds scurrying, John Tapiner had been a little slow in getting inside a saloon, and a bullet knocked him over dead in the barroom door. Mrs. Anna Roberts, a restaurant keeper, was slain accidentally, it was said, with a bullet meant for a fugitive man. J. A. Nolly was wounded and lay crumpled on the sidewalk, dying later. Deputy Sheriff D.

===Chinese===

在遥远的昨天，它就像今天一样是一条细绳；单层的木板商铺和简陋的酒馆，沿着唯一一条弯弯曲曲的街，在峡谷里排成上下两列；山坡上则糊满了摇摇欲坠地扒着山岩的矿工松木棚屋。在那一带拓荒时代的商家中，生意最兴隆的莫过于戈德沃特与卡斯特尼达杂货店。
1883年12月8日夜里七点半，丹·多德和雷德·桑普尔两个一身牛仔行头、面目粗悍的家伙，站在戈德沃特与卡斯特尼达的店门前。圣诞采购季正酣，比斯比的主街被路灯和商店橱窗里透出的光照得通明。
“都齐了？”丹·多德从嘴角里挤出一句。
“齐了。”雷德·桑普尔回答。
“那就动手。”
于是这两个面目凶悍的家伙从腰间的枪套里拔出六响枪，一个朝街那头开火，一个朝街这头开火。
片刻之前，这条街不妨说是满的。片刻之后，这条街不妨说是空的。人行道上的顾客跑进店里。店里的顾客虽然好奇，也待着不出来了。丹·多德朝街那头一路打，雷德·桑普尔朝街这头一路打。
他们的子弹在人行道上唱着圣诞颂歌的时候，另外三个朋友兼同伙——特克斯·霍华德、比尔·德莱尼和丹·凯利——正在店里用六响枪采购圣诞货物。店主、店员和顾客双手举在空中，沿墙排成一列；这三个人洗劫了钱柜，翻遍了保险箱，抢走了陈列匣里的手表和首饰，把一切扫进一只麻袋，回到街上与同伙会合。
与此同时，丹·多德和雷德·桑普尔也没闲着。第一轮枪声把街上人群吓散时，约翰·塔平纳躲进酒馆慢了一步，一颗子弹把他打死在酒吧间门口。饭馆老板娘安娜·罗伯茨太太，据说死于一颗本要打一个逃跑男人的流弹。J. A. 诺利受了伤，蜷在人行道上，后来死了。副治安官

===Original===

Tom Smith and James Kriegbaum, indignant that five bandits should overawe an entire town of able-bodied citizens, stepped out in the street and for a while turned the one-sided affair into a battle. Smith was quickly killed. Kriegbaum wounded Red Sample in the arm. With three citizens dead and one dying, the five robbers swung on their horses and went galloping down the canyon, terrorizing the lower town with revolver shots. When they rode out of the mountains at the south end of the canyon, they turned east and headed across Sulphur Springs Valley for the Chiricahuas.
This affair was known in the chronicles of the period as the Bisbee massacre.
While the rattle of shots in the lower canyon was still in his ears, Kriegbaum leaped on his horse and in a mad Paul Revere ride across the mountains, reached Tombstone in an hour and a half. Automobilists who travel the up-and-down road today think they are doing pretty well if they cover the distance in fifty minutes. Kriegbaum’s news threw Tombstone into a furore. Deputy Sheriff William Daniels and a posse were soon riding hard for Bisbee. They found the mining town wild with excitement, its streets swarming with armed men. Several posses were already out scouring the country. A number of citizens joined Daniels. Among them John Heath.
Heath had been stirred to the depths of his soul by the red-handed atrocity. Among all the voices lifted against the perpetrators, his was the loudest.
“Damnable outrage,” he declared. “Them fellers deserve no mercy. Track ’em down. Hang ’em to the nearest tree. Them’s my sentiments.”
Where the trail of the robbers turned east at the south end of the canyon, Heath led the posse south.
“Here’s the trail, boys,” he shouted. “They’re headin’ for Mexico.”
The posse clattered after this lynx-eyed fellow who could read a trail in the dark.
Close to the Mexican line, Heath turned west.
“This way, boys,” he called out. “The trail’s gittin’ hot. We’ll ketch them fellers before noon. You bet.”
When morning came, Heath was spurring hard on a trail no one else could see. But no criticism of John Heath, mind you. This honest lad was plainly doing the best he knew how. The posse thought he was wrong﻿—that was all﻿—and back it rode to the mouth of the canyon. Now that it was daylight, the trail of the robbers was plain. It led east.

===Chinese===

……汤姆·史密斯和詹姆斯·克里格鲍姆，愤慨于五个强盗竟能震慑一整座城的全壮丁，冲到街上，一时把这场一面倒的事变成了战斗。史密斯很快丧命。克里格鲍姆打伤了雷德·桑普尔的胳膊。三名市民已死、一名垂死，五名劫匪翻身上了马，沿峡谷疾驰而下，一路鸣枪，吓得下城鸡飞狗跳。出了峡谷南端的山区，他们折向东，穿过硫磺泉谷，朝奇里卡华山而去。
这件事，在当时的大小纪事里被称作“比斯比大屠杀”。
下峡谷的枪声还在耳边，克里格鲍姆就跨上马，保罗·列维尔式地翻山狂奔，一个半钟头赶到了墓碑镇。今天坐汽车走这条七上八下的路的人，五十分钟跑完全程就觉得不错了。克里格鲍姆的消息使墓碑镇炸了锅。副治安官威廉·丹尼尔斯带一队民团很快向比斯比疾驰。他们发现这座矿业城群情激愤，街上满是带枪的人。好几队人马已经出去搜山。一批市民加入了丹尼尔斯，其中有约翰·希思。
希思被这血腥的暴行激荡到了灵魂深处。在所有声讨凶手的嗓门里，他的最响。
“可恶的暴行，”他宣称，“这帮家伙不配得到怜悯。追上他们。把他们吊死在最近的树上。这就是我的看法。”
劫匪的踪迹在峡谷南端折向东的地方，希思却领着民团往南走。
“踪迹在这儿，伙计们，”他喊道，“他们奔墨西哥去了。”
民团丁丁当当跟上这个黑夜里也能认路的夜猫子。
快到墨西哥边境线，希思又转向西。
“这边走，伙计们，”他喊，“踪迹越来越热了。中午以前准能逮住这帮家伙。没错。”
天亮以后，希思还在一条谁也看不见的踪迹上拼命催马。不过，别怪约翰·希思。这个老实小伙子显然是在尽力而为。民团觉得他认错了——仅此而已——便掉头回到峡谷口。天已大亮，劫匪的踪迹清清楚楚，是朝东去的。

===Original===

Somewhere around Soldier Holes in Sulphur Springs Valley, Heath announced that the trail turned north. The others saw that it still ran east. Again Heath was overruled. But merely with deprecatory smiles. He meant well. His mistake was evidently due to his over-anxiety “to ketch them robbers and string ’em up.”
Before the posse reached the Chiricahuas, Heath attempted to lead it off on several other false scents. Vague suspicion gradually took form. It began to look as if a pretty crafty gambler was overplaying his hand.
In the Chiricahuas the five horses the robbers had ridden were found dead at the bottom of a chasm. When the animals had given out from hard riding, the bandits had driven them over a precipice after stealing fresh mounts from a ranch. The tragedy of the five horses was a heartless piece of deviltry. The animals could have been turned loose with no danger to the robbers. A little deeper in the mountains, the pursuit ended. The fugitives had scattered.
When Heath had returned to Bisbee, he was still vociferous in anathema.
“Them bandits didn’t have no call to kill citizens the way they done,” he said. “Ef I’d run on to ’em, I’d shot ’em down like dogs.”
After one of these tirades, Frank Buckles measured Heath with cold eyes.
“Those five fellows,” said Buckles, “stopped at my ranch in Pole Bridge Canyon two nights before the robbery. And, Mr. Heath,” he added, “you were with them.”
Heath was arrested and placed in jail at Tombstone. It was established later that he had planned the robbery. Just a shade too much of enthusiasm had landed Heath in the shadow of the gallows.
Deputy Sheriff Daniels traced Dan Dowd into Chihuahua. The two men met one day on the street of Corralitos, a little mining town in the Sierra Madre Mountains.
“Hello, Dan,” said Daniels.
“Hello, Bill,” said Dowd.
But Daniels spoke over the barrel of a forty-four and Dowd returned the greeting with his hands in the air. Extraditing a criminal from Mexico was slow work in those days. So Daniels smuggled his prisoner across the line, and Dowd was soon in a cell in Tombstone. Delaney was arrested in Minas Prietas in Sonora by a Mexican officer. Without extradition papers, Daniels brought the prisoner over the border in a box car. Kelly was arrested in Deming in a barber’s chair while his face was covered with lather. The barber had identified him by a photograph.

===Chinese===

到了硫磺泉谷的索杰霍尔斯一带，希思宣布踪迹转向北了。其他人明明看见它仍在朝东走。希思再一次被否决。不过只换来几声赔笑。他是好意。他的错，显然要归过于他求擒心切，“非要逮住那帮强盗，把他们吊起来”。
民团还没到奇里卡华山，希思又几次三番想把它引上别的假踪迹。模糊的疑心渐渐有了形状。看来，是一个相当狡猾的赌棍把戏演过了头。
在奇里卡华山里，人们在一道深渊底下发现了劫匪骑的五匹马的尸体。马被跑垮之后，强盗们从一个牧场偷了新马，便把旧马赶下了悬崖。五匹马的惨剧是一桩狠毒的恶行。这些牲口本可以放开，对劫匪毫无危险。再往山里走一点，追捕便告结束。逃犯们已四散而去。
希思回到比斯比之后，咒骂起来依然嗓门洪亮。
“那帮强盗杀老百姓，杀得毫无道理，”他说，“我要是撞上他们，准把他们像狗一样崩了。”
这样一顿痛骂之后，弗兰克·巴克尔斯冷冷地上下打量希思。
“那五个人，”巴克尔斯说，“抢劫前两天在我木桥峡谷的牧场待过。还有，希思先生，”他补了一句，“你当时跟他们在一起。”
希思被捕，关进了墓碑镇的监狱。后来查明，这桩劫案正是他策划的。过分多出的一分热心，把希思送到了绞架的阴影之下。
副治安官丹尼尔斯一路追丹·多德追进了奇瓦瓦。一天，两人在马德雷山脉小矿业镇科拉利托斯的街上碰了面。
“你好啊，丹。”丹尼尔斯说。
“你好，比尔。”多德说。
只是丹尼尔斯说话时隔着一把四四口径的枪管，多德举着双手回礼。当年从墨西哥引渡罪犯是桩慢差事。于是丹尼尔斯把囚犯偷偷运过了界，多德很快住进了墓碑镇的牢房。德莱尼在索诺拉的米纳斯普列塔斯被一名墨西哥军官逮捕。没有引渡文书，丹尼尔斯把囚犯装进一节棚车运过了边境。凯利在德明被捕时正坐在理发椅上，满脸涂着肥皂沫。理发师对着照片认出了他。

===Original===

Red Sample and Tex Howard arrived in Clifton with plenty of money and cut a splurge in underworld resorts. Howard, in princely fashion, bestowed a gold watch upon a woman. Vain of the gift, the siren exhibited it to an admirer who examined it with eyes green with jealousy but not too green to identify the timepiece, by description sent broadcast, as having been part of the loot of the Bisbee massacre. So the enamoured Howard did not bask long in beauty’s smiles. The jealous lover betrayed him, and Howard and Red Sample, shackled hand and foot, took the trail for Tombstone.
Dowd, Sample, Howard, Kelly, and Delaney were tried together before Judge D. H. Pinney in Tombstone and sentenced to hang. Their execution was set for March 28th. Heath, tried separately, was convicted of murder in the second degree, and on February 21st, sentenced to life imprisonment. Bisbee had viewed the hanging verdict for the five robbers with deep-seated satisfaction, but it burst into a storm of rage when it learned that Heath had escaped the gallows. A mass meeting was held. Citizens thundered indignation. Resolutions were passed. And next morning, while the dawn of Washington’s Birthday was red over the Mule summits, a committee of fifty Bisbee men, mounted and armed, rode over the divide on the road to Tombstone.
Tombstone had hardly rubbed the sleep out of its eyes when the Bisbee horsemen rode into town. The streets were empty. Only a few stores had opened. A few housewives cooking breakfast caught a glimpse of the cavalcade. A few drowsy citizens drawing on their trousers stared out the windows wonderingly as it passed. The Bisbee men dismounted at the courthouse. A knock sounded on the iron portal of the jail. Swinging open the door, Sheriff Ward looked into the barrels of several six-shooters.
“Well,” he gasped in astonishment, “I thought it was the Chinaman bringing breakfast for the prisoners.”
Sheriff Ward surrendered the keys, expecting a wholesale lynching. But Heath alone was taken from his cell. The five robbers under sentence of death were in adjoining cells. They were relieved when the committee paid no attention to them. They were justly doomed. These men of Bisbee had come to do justice in the single case in which they were convinced the Court had erred. Heath, in their opinion, was as guilty as the others. He, too, deserved to die.

===Chinese===

雷德·桑普尔和特克斯·霍华德来到克利夫顿时腰缠万贯，在下等场所大摆阔气。霍华德以王公气派，把一块金表赏给了一个女人。这妖姬得了礼物好不得意，拿给一个爱慕者看；那人眼里嫉火烧得发绿，却还没有绿到认不出这块表——凭四方传布的失物清单，它正是比斯比大屠杀的赃物之一。于是热恋中的霍华德没在美人笑靥里泡多久。吃醋的情人出卖了他，霍华德和雷德·桑普尔被锁上手脚，踏上了解往墓碑镇的路。
多德、桑普尔、霍华德、凯利和德莱尼在墓碑镇一同受审于D. H. 平尼法官，被判绞刑，定于3月28日行刑。希思另行受审，被定为二级谋杀罪，2月21日判处终身监禁。比斯比听到五名劫匪被判绞刑的消息，从心底里称快；可一听说希思逃过了绞架，登时掀起一场怒潮。群众大会召开了。市民们雷霆般地表达义愤。决议通过了。第二天清晨，华盛顿诞辰日的曙光染红骡山山脊时，一支五十人的比斯比人马，骑马携枪，翻过分水岭，踏上了去墓碑镇的路。
比斯比骑士们进城时，墓碑镇还没睡醒。街上空空荡荡。只有几家店铺开了门。几个做早饭的主妇瞥见了这支马队。几个睡眼惺忪、正在套裤子的市民隔窗望着它走过，满脸纳罕。比斯比人在法院前下了马。监狱的铁门响起敲击声。治安官沃德拉开门，望进好几支六响枪的枪口。
“咦，”他惊得直喘，“我还当是中国佬给囚犯送早饭来了。”
沃德治安官交出了钥匙，等着一场大屠杀式的私刑。但只有希思一人被带出牢房。被判死刑的五名劫匪关在隔壁牢房，见这队人对自己毫不在意，松了口气。他们罪有应得。这些比斯比人来，是要在唯一一桩他们认定法院判错了的案子里主持正义。在他们看来，希思同其他人一样有罪。他也该死。

===Original===

Heath was led to a telegraph pole north of the courthouse in Tough Nut Street. He stood there in the early morning sunlight as cool and unperturbed as if being lynched were habitual before-breakfast routine, like lemon in hot water or orange juice.
“Got anything to say before you go, Heath?”
“I wish,” replied Heath, “you’d promise not to shoot my body full of holes after you swing me up.”
“All right. We’ll promise you that.”
Heath drew a red bandana from his pocket and bound it around his head so that it hid his face. A hanging had certain punctilios of etiquette to be observed. It was the correct thing for men who went into the other world at a rope’s end to depart with their faces covered. His handkerchief took the place of the official black mask. When the noose had been slipped about his neck, he sensed a certain ineptness. He was fastidious in this matter of dying.
“Shove the knot around under my left ear,” he said through his handkerchief.
“Thank you,” he acknowledged politely when his request had been complied with.
The Bisbee horsemen rode out of town as quietly as they had come. Perhaps not fifty people in Tombstone knew that anything unusual had taken place. Citizens going to their work saw with surprise a dead man hanging twenty feet in air against the telegraph pole in Tough Nut Street. They noticed curiously a placard fastened just below his feet. It read:

> **John Heath**

Was hanged to this Pole

By the

Citizens of Cochise County

For Participation as a Known Accessory in the Bisbee Massacre

At 8:20 **a.m.** February 22, 1884

Washington’s Birthday

===Chinese===

希思被押到法院以北“硬坚果”街上的一根电线杆前。清晨的阳光里他站在那里，又冷静又安详，仿佛被私刑绞死是他早饭前的例行公事，如同热水兑柠檬或橘子汁。
“走之前有什么要说的吗，希思？”
“我希望，”希思答道，“你们答应我，把我吊上去之后，别把我身上打得都是窟窿。”
“好。这个我们答应你。”
希思从口袋里掏出一块红大手帕，缠在头上，蒙住了脸。绞刑自有几条礼数要讲究。上绞索去另一个世界的人，遮着脸走，方为得体。他的手帕顶了官定黑头罩的缺。绳套套上脖颈后，他觉得有点不熨帖。在死这件事上，他是讲究人。
“把绳结推到我左耳朵底下。”他隔着帕子说。
“多谢。”要求照办之后，他客气地道了一声。
比斯比骑士们进城时悄无声息，出城时也一样。墓碑镇上或许不到五十个人知道出了事。上工的市民惊愕地看到，“硬坚果”街的电线杆上，离地二十英尺的空中吊着一个死人。他们好奇地注意到他脚下方钉着一块告示牌，上面写着：

> **约翰·希思**
>
> 被吊死在此电杆
>
> 行刑者：
>
> 科奇斯县公民
>
> 罪名：作为已知从犯参与比斯比大屠杀
>
> 时间：1884年2月22日上午8时20分
>
> 华盛顿诞辰日

===Original===

The Heath lynching resulted in a famous coroner’s verdict. The jury was confronted with a delicate problem. The usual verdict of “death at the hands of parties unknown” was impracticable. All the lynchers were known. But the jury had no intention of naming the parties at whose hands death had occurred; Tombstone heartily approved the hanging bee. Dr. George Goodfellow in his testimony hinted at a way out of the dilemma. He declared death had been caused by emphysema. The jurors had not the slightest idea what emphysema meant, but they caught at the learned word as drowning men at a straw. Dr. Goodfellow explained that emphysema was swelling caused by air in the cellular tissues and was sometimes due to strangulation and sometimes to the effects of high altitude. What more could any honest jury want? The verdict read:

> We, the jury of inquest impanelled and sworn by the coroner of Cochise County, after viewing the body and hearing the testimony, find that the name of the deceased was John Heath, 32 years old, a native of Texas, and that he came to his death from emphysema of the lungs, which might have been, and probably was, caused by strangulation, self-inflicted or otherwise, as in accordance with the medical evidence.

===Chinese===

希思私刑案留下了一份著名的验尸裁定。陪审团面对一道微妙的难题。惯常的“死于身份不明者之手”的裁定行不通。动私刑的人个个有名有姓。可陪审团绝无点名的意思；墓碑镇由衷赞许这场绞刑集会。乔治·古德费洛医生在证词里暗示了一条两全的出路。他宣称，死亡由肺气肿引起。陪审团对肺气肿是什么毫无概念，却像溺水者抓稻草一样抓住了这个学术字眼。古德费洛医生解释说，肺气肿是蜂窝组织里进气造成的肿胀，有时起于扼勒，有时起于高原效应。这样诚实的陪审团还能再要求什么呢？裁定写道：

> 我等验尸陪审团，由科奇斯县验尸官召集并宣誓，查验尸体、听取证词后认定：死者名约翰·希思，32岁，得克萨斯人，死于肺气肿；此症可能——而且多半——由扼勒所致，自为或他为其，均依医学证据。

===Original===

A gallows built for five stood back of the Tombstone courthouse. The high board fence about the yard shut it off from the view of the public. A thrifty citizen erected a grand stand from which the hanging might be witnessed. There seemed prospect of his making considerable money. One morning, when he went to put the finishing touches on the structure, he found it a mass of wreckage. Tombstone did not approve of turning the tragedy into a box-office attraction.
Two miners sat on the dump of the Lucky Cuss mine on the hill back of town. Far below, they could see the courthouse yard, the yellow pine gallows, the strong crossbar above it, the five dangling hempen nooses. The door of the jail opened. Dowd, Sample, Howard, Kelly, and Delaney walked out, herded by deputies, Sheriff Ward at their head. The little procession crossed the yard and mounted the gallows stairs. The five condemned men took their places in line on the trap.
“I’ve knowed all them boys well,” said one of the miners on the Lucky Cuss dump. “Fine fellers as you’d want to meet.”
“Hard to understan’,” remarked the other. “Five husky young fellers. Full of life this minute. Dead as this chunk o’ rock five minutes from now.”
“Had many a drink with them boys. Been to the Bird Cage with ’em. Set in poker games with ’em. Shore wuz nice boys.”
“Look like soldiers standin’ up thar. Heads up. Shoulders back. Game, all right.”
“Yes, sir, knowed ’em well. Set and talked with ’em many’s the time jest like I’m settin’ here talkin’ to you. Dan Dowd played as purty a game o’ bank as you ever seen. Shore knowed how to handle chips.”
“What’re them deputies doin’?”
“Stroppin’ their hands behind their backs and stroppin’ their ankles together. That’s the way they do. Old pals o’ mine, all them fellers. Spent their money free. Many’s the﻿—”
“That’s the black caps, ain’t it? They’re slippin’ on the nooses. The sheriff’s shootin’ them knots tight agin their necks. What’s that deputy steppin’ back to the rail fer?”
“He’s goin’ to cut the string. Good night. Thar she goes.”
“God!”
“Done dropped ’em through. Well, I’ll be damned. And jest to think I knowed all them boys well. Yes, sir, knowed ’em well.”
“Can’t see ’em. Can’t see nothin’ but a black slit in the floor of the gallows.”
“Jest a black slit. That’s all. And black as hell.”
Into the blackness, five men had dropped to death.

===Chinese===

墓碑镇法院后面立起了一座为五人预备的绞架。院子四周围着高高的板墙，挡住公众的视线。一位精明的市民搭起一座看台，好让人观刑。看来他有望大赚一笔。一天早晨他去给工程收尾，却发现它成了一堆碎木。墓碑镇不赞成把悲剧变成票房生意。
两个矿工坐在镇后山上“幸运儿”矿的废石堆上。他们望得见下方远处法院的院子、黄松木绞架、架上方结实的横梁、五条垂着的麻绳套。监狱的门开了。多德、桑普尔、霍华德、凯利和德莱尼在副手们的看押下走出来，沃德治安官领头。小小的一队人穿过院子，登上绞架的台阶。五名死囚在踏板上站成一排。
“这几个小子我都熟得很，”“幸运儿”废石堆上的一个矿工说，“都是你想要结识的那种好伙伴。”
“真想不通，”另一个说，“五个壮实的小伙子。这会儿还活蹦乱跳。五分钟后就跟这块石头一样死透了。”
“跟这几个小子喝过多少回酒。一块儿去过鸟笼剧院。一块儿打过牌。都是好样儿的。”
“站在那儿倒像当兵的。头抬着。胸挺着。有种，真有种。”
“可不是，熟得很。多少回就这么坐着跟他们聊，就像我这会儿跟你聊一样。丹·多德打银行牌那手，打得漂亮极了。筹码使得门儿清。”
“那几个副手在干啥？”
“把手绑到背后，再把脚脖子捆一块儿。规矩就这样。都是老伙计，那几个小子。花钱大方得很。多少回——”
“那是黑头罩吧？套绳套了。治安官在把绳结紧到脖子上。那个副手退到栏杆边干啥？”
“他要割绳子了。晚安。落地喽。”
“天哪！”
“放下去喽。唉，真见鬼。想想吧，这几个小子我全熟。可不是么，熟得很。”
“看不见了。只看得见绞架地板上一道黑缝。”
“就一道黑缝。没别的。黑得跟地狱一样。”
五个人坠入那片黑暗，坠入了死亡。

===Original===

The Bisbee massacre had been expiated.
Train robberies came into vogue among Arizona outlaws in the ’nineties. Lonely deserts, convenient mountains, and trains bearing fortunes in their safes made the crime attractive. Train robberies at Canyon Diablo, Maricopa, Rock Cut, Willcox, and Stein’s Pass were pioneer successes for daring spirits to emulate. Black Jack Ketchum was one of the most famous of these early-day robbers. After robbing a bank at Nogales, he killed a man of Sheriff Fly’s pursuing posse at Skeleton Canyon, adding one more tragedy to the many tragedies of that famous pass in the Peloncillos. Black Jack, it was supposed, buried much of his loot in a cave in Wild Cat Canyon at the south end of the Chiricahuas near William Lutley’s ranch. He called this cave Room Forty-Four. Captured after a train robbery, Black Jack imparted to Leonard Alvorsen full directions as to how to lift this treasure. But, unfortunately, Alvorsen had no opportunity to go on a treasure hunt, being detained behind bars at Yuma. Falling heir to Alvorsen’s information, Bert and Harry Macia of Tombstone ransacked Room Forty-Four, but no doubloons or pieces-of-eight rewarded them. Black Jack, a Falstaffian robber much given in prosperous days to fat capon and flagons of sack, was sentenced to hang at high noon in Clayton, New Mexico. On the gallows he appeared anxious. “What time is it?” he asked. He was told it lacked one minute of twelve. “Well, hurry up,” urged Black Jack. “I’m due to eat dinner in hell at twelve sharp.” The drop, it is said, severed the giant’s head from his body.
They still laugh in Arizona at the silver-rain train robbery at Willcox in 1895. Grant Wheeler and Joe George, the bandits, found $60,000 in Mexican silver dollars stacked in sacks in the express car. This money was too bulky to carry away, and they used it to weigh down the dynamite they placed against the safe. The explosion wrecked the car. The dollars went flying through the roof and on toward the stars, and fell in a silver rain over all the landscape. Telegraph poles were shot full of coins, which later were scraped off like barnacles, and a pile of crossties beside the track was turned into a silver mine. Searchers worked for days picking up the money, some of the silver pieces being found a half mile from the railroad, and, strangely enough, all but $600 was recovered.

===Chinese===

比斯比大屠杀的血债清偿了。
抢劫火车，在十九世纪九十年代的亚利桑那不法之徒中成了时兴的营生。荒凉的大漠，近便的群山，再加上保险柜里载着大笔钱财的火车，使这门罪行颇具吸引力。峡谷魔鬼、马里科帕、岩口、威尔科克斯和斯坦斯山口几起劫车，为胆大妄为之辈开创了仿效的先例。“黑杰克”凯彻姆是这些早期劫匪中最有名的之一。在诺加利斯抢过一家银行之后，他在骷髅峡谷打死了弗莱治安官追捕队中的一人，给佩隆西约山中那座著名山口的诸多惨案又添了一桩。据信，黑杰克把大部分赃物埋在奇里卡华山南端、威廉·勒特利牧场附近的野猫峡谷的一个山洞里。他管这洞叫“四十四号房”。一次劫车被捕后，黑杰克把起获这批宝藏的详细路径全数传授给了伦纳德·阿尔沃森。可惜阿尔沃森没有机会去寻宝，他给扣在尤马的铁窗后头。继承了阿尔沃森情报的墓碑镇伯特、哈里·马西亚兄弟把“四十四号房”翻了个底朝天，却没得到一枚多布隆或八里亚尔银币。黑杰克，这位得意日子里离不了肥阉鸡和大壶塞克酒的福斯塔夫式强盗，被判处在新墨西哥州克莱顿正午时分绞决。上了绞架他显得焦躁。“几点了？”他问。人家告诉他差一分十二点。“那好，快点，”黑杰克催促道，“我十二点整要在地狱吃饭。”据说，那一坠把这位巨人的脑袋从身上扯了下来。
亚利桑那至今还在笑1895年威尔科克斯那场“银雨”劫车。劫匪格兰特·惠勒和乔·乔治在快运车厢里发现6万元墨西哥银元，一袋袋码得整整齐齐。这些钱太沉，带不走，他们便拿来压在安在保险柜上的炸药上。爆炸掀翻了车厢。银元穿透车顶飞向星空，化作一阵银雨落满四野。电线杆被银币打得像长了藤壶，后来像刮藤壶一样刮了下来；铁轨旁一堆枕木变成了银矿。寻钱的人捡了好几天的银币，有的落在离铁路半英里外；说来奇怪，除了600元，其余全都收了回来。

===Original===

The two robbers got only $1,500 from the safe and were trailed by bills with corners singed off which they spent in saloons during their flight. Joe George was caught and sent to Yuma. Billy Breakenridge, Sheriff Behan’s famous deputy, who was now Detective William Breakenridge of the Southern Pacific Railroad, tracked Grant Wheeler to Mancos, Colorado. There, when the robber was cornered without hope of escape, he blew out his brains.
Burt Alvord, Sheriff Slaughter’s old deputy, was living during this period at Willcox. He owned a cattle ranch, took a hand in politics, and had been elected constable. But he was the same genial roistering, devil-may-care fellow as in old Tombstone days, and everybody liked him. Living in Willcox, too, was William Downing, a cattleman reputed rich and looked upon as an honest, substantial citizen. But Downing had been a member of the notorious Sam Bass band of outlaws in Texas, had served two terms in the penitentiary, and had been run out of Texas by the Rangers. Downing and Burt Alvord became friends, and this sinister friendship marked the turning point in Alvord’s career.
The Southern Pacific express, westbound, was robbed at Cochise southwest of Willcox in September, 1899. Two men flagged the train at midnight, overawed the train crew with six-shooters, dynamited the safe, and escaped with $10,000. Sheriff Scott White with a posse, of which Burt Alvord was a member, pursued the robbers into the Chiricahuas, where the trail was lost. No clue to the identity of the bandits was discovered. They had vanished into mystery, and the Cochise robbery passed into history as one of the most skilfully managed criminal enterprises that had ever occurred in Arizona. It was not until six months later that Jeff Milton turned the light of a blazing shotgun on the secret history of this interesting affair.
Jeff Milton of Fairbank, a famous fighting man of the Tombstone country, was born in Florida in 1861. He has been a part of the Southwest since his youth, as Texas Ranger, chief of police of El Paso, cattle association detective, railroad detective, and shotgun messenger, and for years immigration agent and customs inspector along the border between Arizona and Mexico.

===Chinese===

两个劫匪从保险柜里只得到1500元，逃亡途中在酒馆里花出去的钞票，票角都带着烧焦的痕迹，成了追缉的线索。乔·乔治被捉，送进了尤马。比恩治安官那位著名的副手比利·布雷肯里奇——如今是南太平洋铁路的侦探威廉·布雷肯里奇——一路追踪格兰特·惠勒到科罗拉多州的曼科斯。在那里，走投无路的劫匪轰碎了自己的脑袋。
斯劳特治安官的老副手伯特·奥尔沃德，这一时期正住在威尔科克斯。他拥有一座牧场，插手政治，还当选了警务官。但他还是老墓碑镇年代那个爱热闹、乐天不怕事的汉子，人人都喜欢他。同样住在威尔科克斯的还有威廉·唐宁，一个牧牛人，据说很有钱，被视为诚实可靠的体面公民。可是唐宁当年在得克萨斯曾是恶名昭彰的萨姆·巴斯匪帮的成员，坐过两回大牢，被骑警队赶出了得克萨斯。唐宁和伯特·奥尔沃德成了朋友，这段凶险的友谊，成了奥尔沃德一生的转折点。
1899年9月，南太平洋铁路西行的快车在威尔科克斯西南的科奇斯站被劫。两个人半夜拦停了火车，用六响枪镇住车组，炸开保险柜，带着1万元逃走。治安官斯科特·怀特带一队民团——伯特·奥尔沃德也在其中——追进奇里卡华山，踪迹在那里断了。劫匪的身份没有发现任何线索。他们消失在迷雾里，科奇斯劫车案作为亚利桑那有史以来办得最漂亮的犯罪大案之一载入史册。直到六个月后，杰夫·米尔顿才用一杆喷火的霰弹枪，照亮了这桩趣事秘史的角落。
费尔班克的杰夫·米尔顿，墓碑镇一带著名的枪手，1861年生在佛罗里达。他自少年起就是西南部的一分子：当过得克萨斯骑警、埃尔帕索警察局长、牧牛业协会侦探、铁路侦探和押运枪手，还多年在亚利桑那与墨西哥边界上做移民官和海关稽查员。

===Original===

Gray of hair and moustache, the old fire-eater is still a gallant figure and, for all his years on the frontier, still retains the manners, speech, and kindly courtesy, touched with a certain courtliness, that belong to the tradition of the Old South.
The train from Nogales to Benson pulled into Fairbank at dusk on a February day in 1900. Jeff Milton, express messenger, was leaning in the doorway in the side of the express car, his shotgun in the crook of his arm. He smiled when he saw a half-dozen citizens lined up on the station platform with two cowboys behind them with drawn six-shooters. The idea of a train robbery did not occur to him. It was pretty dark, and he thought the cowboys, half-tipsy perhaps, were having fun with some tenderfeet. He chuckled aloud when one of the cowboys shouted, “Git up your hands.” But when he saw a cowboy, using the row of citizens as breastworks, point a six-shooter in his direction, he woke up. That gun looked a little like business. Maybe, after all, this was the real thing.
“Come out of that car,” yelled the cowboy.
“If there’s anything in this car you want,” Milton flung back, “come and get it.”
Flame leaped from the cowboy’s gun. The bullet shattered the bone in Milton’s arm and knocked him over on the car floor. The cowboy rushed for the door. He was climbing in the car when Milton got to his knees, one arm dangling like a rag, and raising his shotgun with one hand, fired. The robber tumbled back on the ground with a load of buckshot in his body.
“Look out, boys,” he shouted, “this fellow’s shootin’ to kill. He’s got me.”
“I slammed the door shut and locked it,” said Milton. “Then, as there was no way for those fellows to get in unless they dynamited the car, I staggered over to a piece of baggage and sat down. An orchestra began to play. Thinks I, ‘That’s the most beautiful music I ever heard in my life.’ The next thing I knew, a setter pup was licking me in the face and Baca, the engineer, was standing over me.”
Milton’s heroic duel saved a valuable treasure in the safe and ended the train robbery. The man he had shot was Three-fingered Jack Dunlap. The other bandits were Bravo John Yoes, Bob Brown, and Jack and Lew Owens. Three-fingered Jack was being held in the saddle when the robbers rode off in the darkness.

===Chinese===

头发和髭须都灰白了，这位老斗士依然仪表堂皇；在边疆过了这么多年，他仍然保留着南方老派的传统：那份带几分古雅的言谈举止和仁厚的客气。
1900年2月的一天黄昏，从诺加莱斯开往本森的火车驶进费尔班克。快运押运员杰夫·米尔顿斜倚在快运车厢侧门口，臂弯里抱着霰弹枪。他看见站台上站着一排市民，六七个，身后是两个拔出六响枪的牛仔，不由笑了。他压根没想到劫车。天色相当暗，他当是那两个牛仔——也许带了三分酒意——在捉弄几个菜鸟。一个牛仔喊“举起手来”，他还出声笑了。可等到看见一个牛仔借着那排市民做掩体，把六响枪指向他这边，他醒了。那杆枪看着有点像正经买卖。没准儿，这回是真的。
“从车厢里出来。”牛仔吼道。
“这车厢里要有你想要的东西，”米尔顿回敬，“自己来拿。”
牛仔的枪口喷出火光。子弹打碎了米尔顿臂上的骨头，把他掀倒在车厢地板上。牛仔向车门扑来。他正往车里爬，米尔顿单膝撑起身子，一条胳膊像破布一样耷拉着，单手端起霰弹枪开火。劫匪带着满身大号铅砂仰面栽倒在地。
“小心，伙计们，”他喊道，“这家伙是玩真格的。我中招了。”
“我砰地关上门，落了锁，”米尔顿说，“这下他们进不来了，除非炸车厢。我摇晃着挪到一件行李上坐下。一支乐队奏起了乐。我心想，‘这是我这辈子听过最好听的曲子。’再接下来，一只塞特小狗在舔我的脸，司机巴卡站在我跟前。”
米尔顿这场英雄的独斗，保住了保险柜里一笔可观的财宝，也终结了这场劫车。他打伤的人是“三指杰克”邓拉普。其余匪徒是“好汉约翰”约斯、鲍勃·布朗、杰克·欧文斯和卢·欧文斯兄弟。劫匪们遁入黑暗时，三指杰克是被架在马鞍上驮走的。

===Original===

Milton was rushed in a caboose drawn by a special engine to Tucson where surgeons removed several inches of bone from his upper arm. The wound left him a cripple for life.
Sheriff Scott’s posse found Three-fingered Jack, half dead and abandoned by his comrades, at Buckshot Springs in the Dragoons and took him to Tombstone where he was placed in the hospital. Bob Brown and the two Owens brothers were arrested a few days later. Bravo John was taken at Cananea in Mexico. Three-fingered Jack died six days after his capture. On his deathbed, he told the story of the Cochise train robbery. Burt Alvord and William Downing, he declared, had planned the crime; Billy Stiles, a constable at Pierce, and Matt Burts, a cowboy, had committed the robbery. Alvord, Downing, and Stiles were soon in jail at Tombstone, and Burts was arrested in Wyoming. For a time Willcox and Tombstone refused to believe two such respectable citizens as Alvord and Downing were implicated in a train robbery. Then Billy Stiles confessed. He and Burts, Stiles said, had turned over the $10,000 taken at the robbery to Alvord and Downing. These “master minds” of the conspiracy, according to Stiles, had made a masterful division of the spoils, giving Stiles only $480 as his share. This niggardliness had rankled with Stiles, and his confession was the outcome of the grudge he had nursed.
But having betrayed his accomplices, Stiles suffered from remorse. Permitted a certain amount of liberty as a state’s witness, he held up the Tombstone jailer, whom he shot in the leg, and getting possession of the keys, released Alvord and Bravo John. Arming themselves with guns taken from the sheriff’s office, the three men rushed from the jail. At the door they met Matt Burts, back from Wyoming in custody of a deputy sheriff. Burts joined them, and the four outlaws galloped out of town on stolen horses. Downing had refused to join in the jail break.
Stiles, Bravo John, and Burts were recaptured, and after two years of liberty spent in Sonora, Alvord surrendered to Sheriff Del Lewis, an old friend, at Naco. But, tiring of imprisonment, Alvord and Stiles escaped from the Tombstone jail a second time in 1903 by tunnelling through the walls. Both were recaptured, and at last all the men concerned in the Cochise and Fairbank robberies went to Yuma prison.

===Chinese===

米尔顿被一节专车头拉着的守车火速送往图森，外科医生从他的上臂取出了好几英寸骨头。这处伤使他终身残疾。
治安官斯科特的民团在德拉贡山的巴克肖特泉找到了半死不活、被同伙抛弃的三指杰克，把他送到墓碑镇，安置进医院。几天后，鲍勃·布朗和欧文斯兄弟落网。“好汉约翰”在墨西哥的卡纳内阿被抓。三指杰克被俘六天后死去。弥留之际，他讲出了科奇斯劫车案的内情。他声明，策划此案的是伯特·奥尔沃德和威廉·唐宁；动手抢劫的，是皮尔斯的警务官比利·斯泰尔斯和牧牛人马特·伯茨。奥尔沃德、唐宁和斯泰尔斯很快进了墓碑镇的监狱，伯茨在怀俄明被捕。有一阵子，威尔科克斯和墓碑镇都不肯相信奥尔沃德和唐宁这样体面的公民会牵涉劫车。后来比利·斯泰尔斯招了。据斯泰尔斯说，他和伯茨把劫来的1万元交给了奥尔沃德和唐宁。照斯泰尔斯的说法，这两位阴谋的“主脑”把赃物做了一番高明的分配，只分给他480元。这份抠门在斯泰尔斯心里结了疙痞，他怀恨告发，便是这疙痞的收梢。
可是出卖了同伙之后，斯泰尔斯又受着良心的煎熬。他作为污点证人享有一限度的自由，竟持枪打劫了墓碑镇的狱吏，打伤其腿，夺了钥匙，放出了奥尔沃德和“好汉约翰”。三人从治安官办公室里抄了枪，冲出监狱。在门口撞见马特·伯茨——他被一名副治安官从怀俄明押解回来。伯茨入了伙，四个亡命之徒骑着偷来的马冲出城去。唐宁拒绝参加越狱。
斯泰尔斯、“好汉约翰”和伯茨再次被捕；奥尔沃德在索诺拉逍遥了两年，最后在纳科向老朋友德尔·刘易斯治安官投案。但狱中岁月难熬，1903年，奥尔沃德和斯泰尔斯掘穿狱墙，第二次逃出墓碑镇监狱。两人再度落网。至此，科奇斯与费尔班克两案的所有人犯，都进了尤马监狱。

===Original===

Stiles was killed in Nevada in 1908 by outlaws whom, it is said, he was planning to betray. Downing was killed by Territorial Ranger Speed at Willcox the same year. Bravo John passed into oblivion in Mexico. After serving two years in the penitentiary, Alvord went to Honduras where, it was said, he married a wealthy coffee-planter’s daughter, and where, according to word that came back to Tombstone, he died in 1912.
Jeff Milton, George Scarborough, and Eugene Thacker had trailed Bronco Bill Waters, Bill Johnson, and Jack Pitkin into the White Mountains in northeastern Arizona and were in camp at McBride’s Crossing on the headwaters of Black River. The three fugitives had robbed a Santa Fe train at Belen, New Mexico, and killed three men of a sheriff’s posse that had gone out from Socorro.
“I was fishing near camp,” said Milton, “and had landed several nice trout when I heard firing. Bronco Bill and his two pals had given us a little surprise. I stuck my fishing pole in the bank and slid my Winchester off my shoulder. I saw Johnson churning his gun behind a juniper two hundred yards away. I clipped him in the hip. The bullet split against the bone, and we buried him next morning. Bronco Bill, who had been fighting from the ground, jumped on his horse. ‘Hold on, Cap,’ I called to him, ‘I’ve got a little business I want to talk over with you.’ But he didn’t stop until I knocked him out of the saddle with a bullet. Pitkin got away. After serving twenty years in the penitentiary, Bronco Bill was pardoned out only a couple of years ago. I met him in Naco. He didn’t look much like the bold, bad train robber I had once nearly killed. He was white-haired and stooped and feeble. Old age,” added Milton philosophically, “seems to treat us all alike. I felt kind o’ sorry for the old devil.”

===Chinese===

1908年，斯泰尔斯在内华达被一伙不法之徒打死——据说他正打算出卖他们。同年，唐宁在威尔科克斯被领地骑警斯皮德击毙。“好汉约翰”在墨西哥湮没无闻。奥尔沃德服满两年苦役后去了洪都拉斯，据说娶了咖啡园主的女儿；据传回墓碑镇的消息，他死于1912年。
杰夫·米尔顿、乔治·斯卡伯勒和尤金·萨克追踪“野马比尔”沃特斯、比尔·约翰逊和杰克·皮特金，追到亚利桑那东北部的怀特山，在布莱克河源头的麦克布莱德渡口扎营。这三个逃犯在新墨西哥州的贝伦劫了一列圣菲铁路的火车，还打死了索科罗出动的民团里的三个人。
“我在营地附近钓鱼，”米尔顿说，“已经钓上好几条不错的鳟鱼，忽然听见枪响。野马比尔和他两个伙计给了我们一点小小的意外。我把钓竿插在岸上，把温彻斯特枪从肩上滑下来。我看见约翰逊在两百码外一棵杜松后面摇着他的枪。我一枪打中他的胯。子弹撞在骨头上裂了花。第二天早上我们把他埋了。一直趴在地上打的野马比尔翻身上了马。‘站住，老兄，’我朝他喊，‘我有点买卖要跟你谈谈。’可他不肯停，直到我一枪把他从鞍上打下来。皮特金跑了。野马比尔在牢里蹲了二十年，也就前几年才获释。我在纳科碰见过他。他看上去可不大像我当年差点打死的那条大胆凶悍的劫车犯了。头发白了，背驼了，人也虚了。”“老年对咱们谁都不偏心，”米尔顿达观地补了一句，“那老坏蛋，我还真有点心疼他。”

## I Hills of Silver / 一 白银之丘
===Original===

Geronimo and his warriors were out of their stronghold in the Dragoons. Through all the southeastern part of Arizona from the San Simon to the Huachucas and from the Gila to the Mexican line, the Apache bands were relentlessly harrying the thinly settled land with a warfare of ambuscade and assassination. Through desert mesquite and cactus, the invisible savages slipped with the noiseless swiftness of running water; like poisonous reptiles they wriggled upon their bellies to points of murderous vantage; as still as cougars and as patient, they lurked beside the trails or watched a cabin door. The sudden cough of a rifle through the golden peace of the sunshine gave the first intimation of their ghostly nearness. A wagon train was ambushed in some pass in the hills; a miner in some lonely gulch was murdered from a cholla thicket; a settler with his wife and children was massacred on his homestead claim. Back to their mountain fastnesses from their slinking, silent war trails, the red butchers bore their spoils﻿—a side of bacon, a sack of flour, a bag of beans, a little tobacco, a pair of overalls, a calico wrapper, a baby’s rattle. Then the riotous feasting, the paeans of drumming tom-toms, the wild, impassioned dance of triumph. This was the Apache way in war. No battles, only murder. If the soldiers pressed hard upon their traces, the savages, on their tough, swift ponies, vanished as by magic through secret mountain paths to safety in old Mexico.
Into this wilderness land of ambushed death, Ed Schieffelin, unknown as yet to fame or fortune, came as a civilian scout in 1877, with a flying column recruited in the Hualapai country on the borders of the Grand Canyon and under the command of Al Sieber, celebrated in Apache campaigns as one of the greatest government scouts of the Southwest. The troopers made their headquarters at Camp Huachuca, a newly established army post at the north end of the Huachuca range, and scoured the country round about for Indians.
Eastward across the San Pedro Valley from the post, a group of high hills cut the skyline. Unlovely hills, treeless, their brown slopes a dreary monotony of huge rocks and boulders among which cactus and greasewood made hard shift to live. A sheer desert tumultuously uplifted and set on edge. There was a look of frank poverty about them. One sweep of the eye took them in.

===Chinese===

杰罗尼莫和他的武士们出了德拉贡山的巢穴。从圣西蒙到胡阿丘卡、从希拉河到墨西哥线的整个亚利桑那东南部，阿帕奇各股以伏击与暗杀的战法，无情形 entertained 稀疏星殖的土地。在荒漠的牧豆树和仙人掌间，看不见的野室像奔水一样无声地滑行；像毒蛇一样肚皮贴地，翻写到杀人的有利位置；像美洲狮一样静、一样前心，伏在道旁，或盯着一间小屋的门。一声步枪的咳嗽穿过金色和平的阳光，是他们鬼魅逼近的头一个通报。一支车队在某处山口遭伏；某个孤沟里的矿工从仙人掌丛中遭杀；某个带着妻儿的移民在自己的宅地上被屠。红色屠夫们顺着隐秘无声的战道回到山間堡垒，驮回战利品——一条腌肉，一袋面粉，一包豆子，一点烟草，一条工装裤，一件印花布袍，一只嬰儿的拨浪鼓。然后是纵情的盛宴，冬冬的鼓声凯歌，狂热的胜利之舞。这就是阿帕奇人的战法：没有战斗，只有谋杀。大兵若追得紧，野室们便跨上吃苦耐劳的快马，像变戏法一样，从隐秘的山径遁入老墨西哥的安全地界。
1877 年，埃德·希费林——当时名利两无——以平民侦察兵的身份进入这片死亡伏伺的荒野之地，随一支飞行纵队；队伍在大峡谷边上的华拉派印第安地区招募，统兵的是阿尔·西伯，西南部最伟大的政府侦察兵之一，在历次阿帕奇征战中大名鼎鼎。骑兵们以胡阿丘卡营为大本营——那是胡阿丘卡山脉北端新建的陆军哨所——四出搜剿印第安人。
从哨所向东隔圣佩德罗谷，一群高丘切在天际线上。丑丘：无树，棕色的山坡上巨石磊磊，单调荒凉，仙人掌和油腻木在其间艰难求生。一片被猛然抬起、立了起来的荒漠。它们带着坦率的穷相。一眼便收尽了。

===Original===

No dark, mysterious gorges, no hidden pockets, no shadowy valleys invited curious investigation. Spaniards of Coronado’s time coming up from Mexico on their quest for the Seven Cities of Cibola had passed them by with only an incurious glance. Scouts had threaded through them, troops of cavalry had circled them, Mexican smugglers had camped in their lower draws. But no one had suspected that their drab pauper’s mantle was only a masquerade hiding one of the continent’s richest treasures. They were hills of silver, veined through and through with ore of fabulous richness, filled to the cactus roots and ready to burst with precious metal. One rip of their sides with a miner’s pick and the hoarded wealth would have come gushing forth in resplendent flood. But their hour had not yet struck. The man with the pick had not yet arrived.
On one of their scouting expeditions from Camp Huachuca, Al Sieber and his men rode through the northern reaches of these hills. Schieffelin’s eye caught the gleam of deep mineral stains on a stone. He dismounted and examined the piece of float intently. Silver! Schieffelin looked up in astonishment at the piled-up ugliness of the range. For one skeptical moment, the squalid ridges seemed a sky-high lie. He shook his head. It was impossible. And yet﻿ ﻿… He dropped the chunk of rock in his coat pocket and rode on after his comrades. He said nothing. But his secret thrilled him like wine. He would be a scout no longer. When the Apache hunt was over, he would turn prospector again and hit the trail for the end of the rainbow.
On his return to Camp Huachuca, Schieffelin rode off alone on his mule to seek his fortune. For a while, he made his headquarters with George Woolfolk, who had just taken up land on Barbacomari Creek, sleeping at Woolfolk’s place at night and going out every day into the hills. He found the spot where he had picked up the float, but he was unable to trace the rock to its source. Falling in with William Griffith and a partner who had come from Tucson to do assessment work on the old Brunckow mine, he was induced to stand guard for them against the danger of Indian attack while they completed their job in the shaft.
The old Brunckow house, a long three-room adobe, stood in a little bowl of a valley a mile east of the San Pedro River. Its history even then was long and romantic.

===Chinese===

没有幽暗神秘的峡谷，没有藏私的口袋，没有阴影沉沉的山谷，勾人探究。科罗纳多时代的西班牙人从墨西哥北上寻找西波拉七城，路过它们，只漫不经心瞥了一眼。侦察兵们从其间穿行过，骑兵队绕着它们巡过，墨西哥走私队在它们的下谷宿过营。可谁也不曾疑心：它们那身灰扑扑的贫衣，不过是化装，遮着这座大陆最富的宝藏之一。它们是白银之丘，通体矿脉纵横，富得离奇，装到仙人掌根、胀到欲裂的贵金属。矿工的镐只要在山腰划开一道口子，窖藏的财富便会异彩奔流，一泻而下。可它们的时辰未到。拿镐的人还没来。
一次从胡阿丘卡营出发的侦察中，阿尔·西伯带人马穿过这些山的北段。希费林的眼睛攝住了一块石头上深色矿物 staining 的闪光。他下马，仔仔细细端详那块转石。银！希费林惊愕地抬头，望向那堆积起来的丑陋山峦。有一瞬，他不敢相信：这片寒碜的山脊简直像个弥天大谎。他摇头。不可能。可是……他把那块石头揣进衣袋，策马赶上伙伴。他一字不提。可这个秘密像酒一样使他周身发热。侦察兵他不做了；等这趟剿印第安的差事一完，他要重操探矿旧业，上彩虹尽头那条路去。
回到胡阿丘卡营，希费林独自骑骡出去碰运气。有一段日子，他以乔治·伍尔福克家为大本营——伍尔福克刚在巴巴科马里溪畔占下土地；他夜里睡在伍尔福克家，白天进山。他找到了拾转石的地点，却追不出矿石的源头。他週上了威廉·格里菲斯及其合伙人——他们从图森来给老布伦科矿做探矿维护——被说服替他们站岗，防印第安人来袭，好让他们把井下的活干完。
老布伦科屋，一长条三间的土坏房，立在圣佩德罗河以东一英里一个小碗形的谷里。它的历史，即便在当时，也已经又长又浪漫。

===Original===

It was built in 1858 by Frederick Brunckow, a native of Berlin, graduate of the University of Westphalia, scholar and scientist who, exiled from Germany because of his activities in the revolution of 1848, had drifted out into these solitudes. Brunckow had begun to dig a mine near the house, and it had reached the depth of a grave when an Indian arrow toppled him over into it dead.
Science on that primitive frontier connoted a sort of magic, and a story spread that the German wizard, by occult divination, had located a vast treasure deep in the earth. So a long succession of adventurers came and dug in the shaft, but death was all any man-jack of them ever found. Two in addition to Brunckow were killed by Apaches, and claim-jumping fights over the worthless hole in the ground brought the toll of dead men, it is said, up to seventeen. But the myth of treasure deeper down still lives, and year after year assessment work is done to hold the claim. Nowadays, the old house and mine are said to be haunted. Travellers along the old Charleston road, it is declared, hear cries and groans at night or see shadowy forms stalking in the moonlight.
Here at the old Brunckow mine, Al Sieber riding up with a party of scouts found Schieffelin sitting on a pile of rock on guard with his rifle across his lap.
“What’re you doin’, Ed?” asked Sieber.
“Prospecting, mostly,” Schieffelin drawled.
“Whar?”
“Over yonder.” Schieffelin waved his hand eastward toward the hills.
“Them hills?” scoffed Sieber. “Thar ain’t nothin’ thar.”
“I’ve picked up some mighty nice-looking stones.”
“All you’ll ever find in them hills’ll be your tombstone,” warned the scout. “Geronimo’ll git you ef you don’t watch out, and leave your bones fer the buzzards to pick.”
“I’ll take a chance,” Schieffelin replied.
His life against a million dollars. That was his chance.
A wide, shallow dry wash leading up from the San Pedro Valley, its sloping sides green with gramma grass and flowering weeds. Ocotillos whose tall, curving, graceful wands springing from a central root were like green jets from a fairy fountain. Chollas armed with needle-sharp spines as thick as bristles on a wild boar, brandishing weirdly deformed arms like truculent devils. Green and yellow mescal plants that shot thirty feet in air skyrocket stalks that exploded in starry white blooms.

===Chinese===

房是 1858 年弗雷德里克·布伦科盖的。他是柏林人，威斯特伐利亚大学毕业，学者兼科学家，因卷入 1848 年革命被德国驱逐，漂流到了这片荒寂之地。布伦科在屋旁开一座矿，井深刚够一座坟，一支印第安箭便把他撂了进去。
在那样的原始边疆，“科学”几乎等于法术；于是流言四起，说这位德国木士靠占卜找到了地底深处的大宝。于是一长串冒险家接踵而来，在井里挖呀挖，可他们每一个人挖到的只有死。继布伦科之后又有两个死在阿帕奇人手里；而为这个不值钱的土窟窿争矿权的火并，据说把死亡人数抬到了十七。可“更深处理着宝”的神话至今活着，年复一年，人们还在做探矿维护好守住矿权。如今，老屋和老矿据说闹鬼。据说走老查尔斯顿道的夜行人听得见哭喊呻吟，或燈见月下的幽灵游荡。
就在老布伦科矿，阿尔·西伯带一队侦察兵骑马过来，发现希费林坐在一堆矿石上持枪警戒，步枪横在膝头。
“你在干啥，埃德？”西伯问。
“主要是探矿。”希费林拖长腔。
“哪儿？”
“那边。”希费林朝东面的山丘一挥手。
“那山？”西伯嗤笑。“那儿啥也没有。”
“我捡了几块挺好看的石头。”
“你在那山里能找到的，只有你自己的墓碑。”侦察兵告诚。“不当心点，杰罗尼莫会抓住你，把你的骨头留给秃鹰劰。”
“我碰碰运气。”希费林答。
一条命换一百万美元——这便是他的赌注。
一条从圣佩德罗谷上来的宽而浅的干河槽，两岸斜坡上gramma草和野花緑油油。刺树那高而弯的柔条，从同一根上抽出，像仙泉喷出的绿泉。仙人掌长满野猪鬃一样密的针刺，挥舞着怪模怪样的臂，像凶悍的小鬼。绿黄的龙舌兰把三十英尺的冲天積射上天空，爆成一团星白的繁花。

===Original===

Schieffelin on his mule, rifle across his saddlebow, travelling through the wash at a walk, looking for stones.
From the yellow, shadowy ramparts of the Dragoons nine miles across the mesquite mesa, a tall slender column of smoke, shimmering darkly in the sun, rose straight into the sky. It broke from its base and drawing slowly upward into space melted from view. A quick, ball-like puff of smoke shot upward like a bursting bomb. Again a slim spiral shorter than the first. Another explosive puff. Another. Once more a brief pillar. Dash.﻿ ﻿… Dot.﻿ ﻿… Dash.﻿ ﻿… Two dots.﻿ ﻿… Dash. Up there somewhere on the mountain wall a half-naked Apache, manipulating a deerskin over a brush fire, was telegraphing a code message to some war party in the valley. A queer little smile twisted the corner of Schieffelin’s mouth. What was that fellow saying? Humph! He tightened a bit his grip on his rifle and went on looking for stones. Find his tombstone? Well, maybe.
He turned a corner of the wash. His mule halted abruptly, ears pricked, forelegs stiffly braced. What was that that gleamed so snowy white among the clumps of bear grass? An outcropping of white rock, perhaps. Or the mouldering skull of some long-dead, crow-bait pony. But no. Schieffelin dismounted. A step forward and there before him lay a human skeleton. Just beyond it another. The sparse grass had laid green tendrils across the glistening shanks. Weeds had shot up between the ribs. A prickly pear was crawling greenly across a disarticulated spinal column. The disjointed bones, bleached to ghastly whiteness by the suns and rains of years, were only slightly out of place here and there, and the two dead men seemed to have lain undisturbed since the moment of sudden tragedy that had overwhelmed them.
The skeletons lay at full length, breast downward, head to head, with the finger bones of the long out-reaching arms almost touching. Between them stood a pile of silver ore perhaps a foot high, the dissevered arm bones almost enclosing it in a glimmering, broken circle. One skull lay turned on its side; the other was firmly imbedded upon its base in the earth, but the dark, hollow eye-sockets of both were trained, as if with conscious intensity, on the little heap of stones that suggested some idol’s shrine before which these ghastly spectres bowed to the ground in unending worship.

===Chinese===

希费林骑在骡上，步枪横在鞍前，在河槽里慢慢走，找石头。
九英里外，隔着牧豆树合地，德拉贡山黄色的、阴影重重的壁垒上空，一柱细高的烟暗暗地在日光里颤，直插天空。烟柱从根上断开，缓缓上升，升入太空，淡出视野。一个球似的烟团骤然上蹿，像炸开的炸弹。又一道比第一道短的细螺。又一团爆烟。又一团。再一柱短柱。划。……点。……划。……两点。……划。上面什么地方的山壁上，一个半裸的阿帕奇人正拿鹿皮在灌木火上扇，向谷里某支战队拍发电报。希费林嘴角扭出一丝古怪的笑。那家伙在说什么？哼！他把枪握紧了些，接着找石头。找他的墓碑？嗯，也许吧。
他拐过河槽的一个弯。骡子猛地站住，双耳直竖，前腿僵僵撑住。那在丝兰丛间白得发光的是什么？也许是一片白岩露头。也许是哪匹死了多年的小马的风干的髑髅。都不是。希费林下了骡。一步上前，一具人的骸骨躺在眼前。再过去，又一具。稀疏的草把绿须铺上白亮的胫骨；杂草从肋骨间钻出来；一株仙人掌正缘着一段散了架的脊椎爬绿。散乱的骨头被多年的日晒雨淋烫成疹人的白，却只稍微错动了几个地方；两个死人仿佛自那场覆灭他们的飞灾降临的一刻起，就不曾受过打搜。
两具骸骨直挺挺趴着，头对着头，伸出的长臂的指骨几乎相碰。两者之间堆着一堆银矿石，约莫一英尺高；断开的臂骨几乎把它围成一圈发亮的残环。一颗髑髅侧躺着；另一颗稳稳地底朝下嵌在土里；可两颗髑髅黑空空的眼窝，都像带着刻意的专注，盯住那小石堆——仿佛什么神像的祭坛，这两具疹人的幽灵便在坛前无休止地俯首礼拜。

===Original===

High above them, on a single stem, a yucca lifted a great cluster of drooping lily-white blossoms that swayed gently in the breeze like a swung censer.
The story of the tragedy that had left these bones to bleach on the desert was as clear as if the skeletons themselves suddenly had sat upright and unfolded every vivid detail. Picture two prospectors beside their camp fire. Rugged men they are, bearded, clear-eyed, ruddy with health. Luck has been with them. They have located a rich ledge of silver during their day’s wanderings. They pour their specimens of ore on the ground. In the red glow of the firelight they gloat over their treasure. Wonderful ore. What will it run? Twenty thousand to the ton? These questions can wait. The assay in Tucson will tell. They pick up the stones, scrutinize them, weigh them in their palms. They are like misers threading fingers joyously through gold. They laugh exultant laughter. But it is growing late. They roll themselves in their blankets and go to sleep under the stars to dream of riches.
But out in the darkness, a devil’s ring has closed around them. While they are slumbering peacefully, fierce eyes keep them all night under baleful surveillance. Apache gods forbid a night attack; the night is sacred to ancestral ghosts. Rosy dawn is a choice time for murder. When morning breaks in rose and gold over the Dragoons, the two men tumble out of bed. For a happy moment they stand facing each other above their pile of ore. They stretch out comradely hands. “Put her there, pardner.” Their fortune’s made. Goodbye to desert hardships. They have struck it rich at last.﻿ ﻿… Apache rifles spit fire. Snaky wisps of blue powder smoke wriggle off across the mesquite.﻿ ﻿…
Schieffelin climbed down off his mule, and stepping gingerly among the bones, examined the pieces of ore one by one. Quick certainty flashed upon him like sunlight. This ore had come from the same source as the float he had found while scouting with Sieber. He was near the treasure for which he had hunted so long. Possibly this spot of dreams was now within the sweep of his vision. But where? He replaced the rocks as he had found them in the half-formed circle between the skeleton arms and rode away, leaving the dead at their eternal salaam before the tiny altar on which they had poured the oblation of their life blood.

===Chinese===

高处，一株丝兰独茎擎起一大簇垂落的百合白花，在微风里轻轻摇曳，像一炉摆荡的香。
这场把白骨留给荒漠暴晒的惨剧，其始末清清楚楚，仿佛骸骨们突然坐起身，把每个细节一一摆开。试想两个探矿人在营火旁。粗析的汉子，络腮胡，眼睛清亮，血色健康。运气在他们这边。一天奔波下来，他们标定了一条富银矿脉。他们把矿石标本倒在地上，在火光的红焰里把玩自己的宝藏。好矿石。能出多少？一吨两万？这些问题不急。图森的化验会说话。他们拾起石头，细看，掂在手心。他们像守财奴把手指喜滋滋地穿进金币。他们扬眉大笑。可是天晚了。他们裹上毯子，在星空下睡去，梦里全是富贵。
可黑暗里，一圈魔环已经套住了他们。他们安睡的时候，凶狠的眼睛整夜恶毒地守着他们。阿帕奇的神不许夜袭；夜是祖灵的圣时。玫瑰色的黎明才是杀人的良辰。朝霞在德拉贡山上金如灿铺开时，两人翻身下“床”。有幸福的一刻，他们隔着自己那堆矿石面对面站着，伸出兄弟般的手。“搭把手，伙伴。”他们发财了。荒漠的苦日子再见。他们终于掘到富矿了。……阿帕奇的步枪吐出火舌。蛇一样的蓝硝烟丝丝缕缕，在牧豆树间漂散而去……
希费林翻身下骡，小心翼翼在骸骨间择路，把矿石一块块检视。阳光般的确定性在他心里一闪。这矿石，同他随西伯侦察时拾到的转石同源。他追了这么久的宝藏，近了。也许，这个梦中之址此刻就在他的视界之内。可在哪儿？他把石头照原样放回骸骨臂弯里那个半成的环中，上马离去，任死者对着他们的小小祭坛水远行礼——坛上，他们已倾尽了生命血的祭。

===Original===

At sunset, Schieffelin, several miles farther up the wash, prepared to camp. He picketed his mule in good grass in a secluded hollow and threw down his blankets on top of a hill a mile away. This was good strategy; a man will lie silent at night in Indian country but a mule may burst into song at any moment.
